    Definition 3.16 (Equivalence of the Canonical Platform). The transaction equivalence of the canon-
 ical platform has the following classes.
 • For a class [𝑡] and a guard 𝑝 of it, 𝑝’s approval class on [𝑡]: 𝑝’s Request transactions of {[𝑡]},
   and its Answer transactions, by either branch, on attempts of [𝑡]. It is guarded by 𝑝.
 • For a guard 𝑝 and a request 𝜌 of 𝑝 of two or more classes, 𝑝’s request class on 𝜌: its Request
   transactions of 𝜌. It is guarded by 𝑝.
 • For a guard 𝑝 and a standing request 𝜌 of 𝑝, 𝑝’s standing class on 𝜌: its Stand transactions of 𝜌;
   and its withdrawing class on 𝜌: its Withdraw transactions of 𝜌. Each is guarded by 𝑝.
 • For a part [𝑡] 𝑗 and an agent 𝑝, 𝑝’s proposing class on [𝑡] 𝑗 : its Propose transactions for attempts
   of [𝑡] 𝑗 .
 • For an agent and a message: its Start, Tally, Yield, Outcome or Readied transactions on that
   message.
 • For an agent and an attempt: its Vote transactions on that attempt, its Commit transactions on
   it, and its Ready transactions on it.
 • For an agent and two attempts: its Wound transactions for them.
    The approval class is what a person wills in CVA when they will [𝑡] in the specification: their
 machine fulfils it by requesting [𝑡] or by answering yes to an attempt of it. A request class is what
 a person wills when they will each class of the request, and a standing class what they will when
 they will each class of the standing request, until they will to withdraw it. The else-branch of
 Answer records the answer no, which no other transaction records, so no else-branch is a machine
 transaction of the platform. It records its attempt, so two Answer transactions with the same
 else-branch are of one class (Definition 2.12).
    Definition 3.17 (Canonical CVA Platform). The canonical CVA platform of a local volition-guarded
 specification is the CVA platform (Definition 3.4) with the cargoes of Definition 3.10 and the
 introductions, the app states of Definition 3.10, initial app state (𝑠 0, 0, ⊥, ∅), the app transactions of
 Definitions 3.12 to 3.15, and the transaction equivalence of Definition 3.16.
    A run of P is correct only where the initiator of an attempt knows its participants (Definition 3.21
 below), and agents come to know each other by Discover, which both persons will, or by an
 introduction from a peer that knows both. Commit introduces the other participants of its attempt
 to each other, so the parties to a transaction taken come to know each other, and a transaction
 refused introduces no one. A canonical platform may also send introductions by a rule of its own.
   Definition 3.18 (Introduction Rule, Introduce Peers). An introduction rule 𝜄 gives, for every agent
 𝑝 and machine state 𝑠 ∈ 𝑆, a finite set 𝜄 𝑝 (𝑠) of two-element sets of agents other than 𝑝, and, for
20                                                                                     Idit Keidar and Ehud Shapiro


every agent 𝑝 and part [𝑡] 𝑗 with 𝑝 ∈ 𝑄 ( [𝑡]), a finite set 𝜄 𝑝 ( [𝑡] 𝑗 ) of such sets. The canonical CVA
platform with 𝜄 is the canonical CVA platform with, for every agent 𝑝 and {𝑞, 𝑟 }, the app transaction
Introduce peers {𝑞, 𝑟 }, unguarded: precondition: {𝑞, 𝑟 } ∈ 𝜄 𝑝 (𝑠), 𝑞, 𝑟 ∈ known𝑝 , and 𝑝 has not
recorded introduced(𝑞, 𝑟 ); effect: 𝑝 adds message(𝑝, 𝑞, intro(𝑟 )) and message(𝑝, 𝑟, intro(𝑞)) to 𝑜 𝑝
and records introduced(𝑞, 𝑟 ); and in which Commit, and Outcome on a commit, of an attempt
of [𝑡] 𝑗 at 𝑝 add the same messages and record for every {𝑞, 𝑟 } ∈ 𝜄 𝑝 ( [𝑡] 𝑗 ) with 𝑞, 𝑟 ∈ known𝑝 for
which 𝑝 has not recorded introduced(𝑞, 𝑟 ). The Introduce peers transactions of an agent for one
pair form a class.
   Introduce peers, and the introductions a commit sends, change the outbox and the record,
and the Introduce they lead to changes the known peers; the specification sees neither, so an
introduction rule leaves the results below as they are.
Correctness. The transaction is carried out, at every participant at once, by the Tally that records
the last yes of its attempt at its initiator and chooses its readings, or by its Propose where it has
one participant: from then on every participant’s effect is settled, and the Commit and Outcome
that apply it later change nothing the specification records.
   Definition 3.19 (Decided, Committed, Aborted, Settled State). Let 𝑐 be a configuration of the
canonical CVA platform over 𝑃. An attempt 𝛼 is decided in 𝑐, with readings 𝑤, if its initiator has
recorded chose(𝛼, 𝑤), having proposed it, voting yes, and recorded a yes of every other participant.
It is closed at a participant 𝑝 once 𝑝 has recorded closed(𝛼), committed there if the transaction
that recorded it applied 𝑝’s effect, and aborted there otherwise. The settled state of 𝑝 in 𝑐 is 𝑠𝑝 , or
 [𝑡 ] 𝑗
𝑓𝑝        (𝑠𝑝 , 𝑤) if 𝐿𝑝 is an attempt of a part [𝑡] 𝑗 decided in 𝑐 with readings 𝑤.
   For the rest of this section fix a local volition-guarded specification, an introduction rule or none,
and agents 𝑃 ⊂ Π, and write P for the transition system of the canonical CVA platform with that
rule over 𝑃 and F (𝑃) for the volitional multiagent transition system the specification induces over
𝑃.
   Proposition 3.20 (Atomic Commitment). In every safe run of P: (agreement) no attempt is
committed at one participant and aborted at another; and (validity) an attempt is committed at a
participant only if it is decided, each participant having voted yes on it holding no other attempt
prepared, at a machine state in the domain of its effect, a reading of which is among those chosen for it,
which meet the condition of its part, and each member of its guard set having voted yes on it with an
approval of its class pending, made by a Request, a Stand or an answer yes, which its person willed.
In every safe, live run of P: (termination) every attempt is eventually closed at each of its participants.
     The proof is in Appendix B.
    Definition 3.21 (Holds, For, Connected, Unobstructed, Resolute, Steady). A part [𝑡] 𝑗 holds in a
configuration if readings of the settled states of the members of 𝑄 ( [𝑡]), one of each, meet its
                                                                        [𝑡 ]
condition, so that the settled state of every 𝑝 ∈ 𝑄 ( [𝑡]) is in dom(𝑓𝑝 𝑗 ), and an agent 𝑝 is for a class
[𝑡] if it holds its approval class on [𝑡], its request class on a request holding [𝑡] or its standing class
on a standing request holding [𝑡], or an approval of [𝑡] is pending at it. A run of P is connected if, for
every part [𝑡] 𝑗 of a class [𝑡] that holds, and all of whose guards are for [𝑡], in every configuration of
a suffix, its opener, if it has one, and otherwise one of its proposers, knows every other participant
of [𝑡] from some configuration on. A run is unobstructed if, for every agent 𝑝, class [𝑡], part [𝑡] 𝑗 of
it, and each of these conditions — 𝑝 is a guard of [𝑡] 𝑗 holding its approval class (Definition 3.16) on
[𝑡], its request class on a request holding [𝑡] or its standing class on a standing request holding
                                                    [𝑡 ]
[𝑡], its settled state (Definition 3.19) in dom(𝑓𝑝 𝑗 ); 𝑝 is a guard of [𝑡] 𝑗 at which an approval of [𝑡]
Volition Elicitation                                                                                  21

                                        [𝑡 ] 𝑗
is pending, its settled state in dom(𝑓𝑝 ); 𝑝 is the opener of [𝑡] 𝑗 , unguarded, its settled state in
      [𝑡 ] 𝑗
dom(𝑓𝑝 ); 𝑝 holds a request of [𝑡] — if infinitely many of its configurations meet the condition,
then 𝑝 is free for [𝑡], and for every class of the request where the condition is of a request class
or a standing class, and clear in infinitely many of them. A run is resolute if no agent takes the
else-branch of Answer on a question of [𝑡] in a configuration in which it holds its request class on
a request holding [𝑡] or its standing class on a standing request holding [𝑡], or an approval of [𝑡] is
pending at it. A run is steady if, for every class [𝑡], if from some configuration on no attempt of [𝑡]
is decided while in every configuration some part of [𝑡] holds and all its guards are for [𝑡], then
one part of [𝑡] holds, and all its guards are for [𝑡], in every configuration of a suffix.
   Definition 3.22 (Strongly Fair, Fairness of P). A run of P is strongly fair if, for every agent 𝑝 and
part [𝑡] 𝑗 of a class [𝑡] such that a Propose of 𝑝 for an attempt of [𝑡] 𝑗 is enabled in infinitely many
of its configurations, 𝑝 takes infinitely many Propose transactions for attempts of [𝑡] 𝑗 , or infinitely
many attempts of [𝑡] are decided; and likewise for every agent 𝑝, class [𝑡] and agent 𝑜 and the
Request transactions of 𝑝 to 𝑜 of requests holding [𝑡], and the Stand transactions of 𝑝 of standing
requests holding [𝑡]. The fairness condition of P is the set of its connected, unobstructed, steady,
resolute and strongly fair runs.
   The protocol cannot secure these conditions. Knowing is never undone, so whether a run is
connected follows from its Discover and Introduce transactions. Unobstructedness excludes an
agent kept busy without end. Resoluteness excludes a person who requests a transaction and
declines it when asked. Steadiness excludes a class, willed and not taken, whose part that holds
with all its guards for it changes without end. The protocol resolves contention: contention makes
an attempt yield only to an attempt with priority over it, and the attempt is proposed again, the
approvals given to it standing. A refused attempt changes no machine state of the specification, and
ends the approvals of the guards that voted on it and the request it names, a volition change of each
of them (Definition B.1): a guard who still wills the transaction wills it again. The specification’s
liveness is weak fairness. For our implementation to achieve it, strong fairness is needed. The
reason is that Propose may be enabled infinitely often but never continuously. The compiled agent
of Section 5.4 secures strong fairness (Section 6.4).
   Definition 3.23 (Implementation of Volition-Guarded Specifications by CVA, Correct, Complete).
The implementation of volition-guarded specifications by CVA is the function taking a local volition-
guarded specification and an introduction rule or none to its canonical CVA platform with that rule
(Definitions 3.17 and 3.18). It is correct if for every such specification and 𝑃 ⊂ Π some 𝜎 is a correct
implementation of F (𝑃) by P, and complete for strongly fair runs if for every such specification and
𝑃 ⊂ Π some such 𝜎 also takes some correct run of P to each correct run of F (𝑃) that is strongly
fair, every class enabled in infinitely many of its configurations being taken in infinitely many.

  Theorem 3.24 (CVA Implementation). The implementation of volition-guarded specifications by
CVA is correct, and complete for strongly fair runs.
   The proof, with the mapping 𝜎, is in Appendix B. Correctness is per transition and then per
class: the yes that decides an attempt is the specification’s transaction, enabled by validity, and
every other step a stutter or a volition change; and a class enabled forever is decided, since, the
run being steady, one of its parts holds with all its guards for the class from some point on and
is proposed again and again, contention aborts its attempts only in favour of the finitely many
attempts with priority over them, and no guard of that part refuses it. Steadiness is needed: with two
parts guarded by Alice with Bob and by Alice with Carol, Alice’s attempt that asks Bob while only
Carol wills fails, and so does her next, which asks Carol while only Bob wills, and the class, enabled
22                                                                                     Idit Keidar and Ehud Shapiro


throughout, may never be taken. Completeness is by construction: for a correct, strongly fair run
of the specification, a run of P in which every agent knows every other carries its transactions out
one at a time, and idles, between them, on the attempts its persons’ unmet wills open.

4     GRASSROOTS LOGIC PROGRAMS
This section recalls GLP [53], the polymorphically-typed multiagent concurrent logic programming
language that vGLP extends (Section 5): its syntax, its reduction, and its multiagent operational
semantics maGLP, which vmaGLP extends with the person. GLP is typed by the parameterised
moded type system of [59], which proved indispensable for AI-based programming and is not
restated here: a program carries type definitions and procedure declarations. Its implementation
is [58].

4.1    Syntax
The language extends logic programs by adding a paired reader 𝑋 ? to every “ordinary” logic variable
𝑋 , now called a writer [53].
   Definition 4.1 (Variables, Terms, Goals, Clauses [53]). Let V denote the set of LP variables (identi-
fiers beginning with uppercase), henceforth called writers, and V? = {𝑋 ? | 𝑋 ∈ V} the readers;
V̂ = V ∪ V?, and a writer 𝑋 and its reader 𝑋 ? form a variable pair. Terms, unit goals, goals, and
clauses are as in LP, over V̂. A term, goal, or clause satisfies the single-occurrence (SO) invariant if
every variable occurs in it at most once, and a clause satisfies the single-reader/single-writer (SRSW)
restriction if it satisfies SO and a variable occurs in it iff its paired variable does.
  Definition 4.2 (Guarded Clause, Procedure, GLP Program [53]). A guarded clause has the form 𝐻 :-
𝐺 | 𝐵, where 𝐻 is the head, 𝐺 a conjunction of guard predicates, and 𝐵 the body; guard arguments
are readers paired to head writers. A GLP program is a finite sequence of guarded clauses, each
satisfying SRSW; the clauses for the same predicate form a procedure. The set of goals Ĝ(𝑀) of a
program 𝑀 includes all goals over V̂ and the vocabulary of 𝑀 that satisfy SO.
  Example 4.3 (Fair Merge [53]). The quintessential concurrent logic program for fairly merging
two streams, in ordinary clauses:1
Stream(X) ::= [] ; [X|Stream(X)].

procedure merge(Stream(X)?, Stream(X)?, Stream(X)).
merge([X|Xs], Ys, [X?|Zs?]) :- merge(Ys?, Xs?, Zs).
merge(Xs, [Y|Ys], [Y?|Zs?]) :- merge(Xs?, Ys?, Zs).
merge([], Ys, Ys?).
merge(Xs, [], Xs?).
The goal merge([1,2,3|Xs?],[a,b|Ys?],Zs) satisfies SO; each clause satisfies SRSW. The first
clause swaps inputs in the recursive call, ensuring fairness when both streams are available.

4.2    Reduction
   Definition 4.4 (Substitutions and Assignments [53]). A writer assignment is a term of the form
𝑋 := 𝑇 , 𝑋 ∈ V, 𝑇 ∉ V, satisfying SO. Similarly, a reader assignment is a term of the form 𝑋 ? := 𝑇 ,
𝑋 ? ∈ V?, 𝑇 ∉ V, satisfying SO. A writers (readers) substitution 𝜎 is the substitution implied by a set
of writer (reader) assignments that jointly satisfy SO. Given a writers assignment 𝑋 := 𝑇 , its readers
1 Parameterised moded-type definitions (T ::= ...) and declarations (procedure ...) used informally in examples are

formally introduced in [59].
Volition Elicitation                                                                                  23


counterpart is 𝑋 ? := 𝑇 , and given a writers substitution 𝜎, its readers counterpart 𝜎? is the readers
substitution defined by 𝑋 ?𝜎? = 𝑋 𝜎. Given a reader assignment 𝑋 ? := 𝑇 , its writers counterpart
is 𝑋 := 𝑇 , and given a readers substitution 𝜏, its writers counterpart 𝜏! is the writers substitution
defined by 𝑋𝜏! = 𝑋 ?𝜏. The pair completion of a readers substitution 𝜎 is 𝜎 ★ = 𝜎 ∪ 𝜎!, applied to a
fixed point.
   Definition 4.5 (Renaming, Renaming Apart, Writer MGU [53]). A renaming is an injective sub-
stitution 𝜌 : V̂ → V̂ such that for each 𝑋 ∈ V: 𝑋 𝜌 ∈ V and 𝑋 ?𝜌 = (𝑋 𝜌)?. Two terms have a
variable in common if for some writer 𝑋 ∈ V, either 𝑋 or 𝑋 ? occurs in both. A renaming 𝜎 renames
𝑇 ′ apart from 𝑇 if 𝑇 ′𝜎 and 𝑇 have no variable in common. Given two unit goals 𝐴 and 𝐻 , a writer
mgu is a writers substitution 𝜎 such that 𝐴𝜎 = 𝐻𝜎 and 𝜎 is most general among such substitutions.
  Definition 4.6 (Reduction [53]). Given a unit goal 𝐴 and an ordinary clause 𝐶, with 𝐻 :- 𝐺 | 𝐵
the result of renaming 𝐶 apart from 𝐴, the reduction of 𝐴 with 𝐶 succeeds with result (𝐵, 𝜎) if 𝐴 and
𝐻 have a writer mgu 𝜎 and 𝐺𝜎? succeeds.
   Guards have three-valued semantics [53]. Each guard predicate explicitly defines its success
condition. A guard suspends if it does not succeed but some instance of it under a readers substitution
would succeed. A guard fails if no such instance exists. A guard conjunction succeeds if all members
succeed; it suspends if any member suspends and none fail; it fails if any member fails. Guard
occurrences count toward SRSW satisfaction, and a guard whose success implies that 𝑋 ? is ground
licenses multiple occurrences of 𝑋 and 𝑋 ? in the clause [53].

4.3    Multiagent Semantics
The operational semantics of GLP is maGLP. Each agent’s initial resolvent is the atomic goal
agent(ch(_?,_),ch(_?,_)), providing two bidirectional channels: the first to the person oper-
ating the machine, the second to the network [53]. Anonymous variables make the initial local
states syntactically identical yet semantically unshared; variable pairs whose writer and reader are
held by different agents are the communication channels, and Cold-call bootstraps shared variables
between previously-disconnected agents through the network streams [53].
   Definition 4.7 (Variables of an Agent [53]). Let V𝑝 ⊂ V, for 𝑝 ∈ Π, be pairwise disjoint infinite
sets of writers. The variables of agent 𝑝 are V̂𝑝 = V𝑝 ∪ {𝑋 ? | 𝑋 ∈ V𝑝 }.
  The anonymous variables of the initial goal at 𝑝, and those a Reduce at 𝑝 renames its clause into,
are variables of 𝑝 [53].
  Definition 4.8 (Goal Identity [53]). Each unit goal in a computation carries a unique identifier
assigned at spawn time: goals in an initial goal receive distinct initial identifiers, and goals spawned
by a Reduce transition receive fresh identifiers. Communicate transitions preserve identifiers: the
goal containing 𝑋 ? retains its identity after instantiation.
  Definition 4.9 (Asynchronous Resolvent [53]). Given a GLP program 𝑀, an asynchronous resolvent
over 𝑀 is a pair (𝐺, 𝜎) where 𝐺 ∈ Ĝ(𝑀) and 𝜎 is a readers substitution.
   Each rule below is read as in an operational semantics: above the line its condition, below it the
transaction, from its participants’ local states to their states after it, every other agent stationary;
the prose before each rule says what the premise names.
   Definition 4.10 (maGLP Transactions [53]). Given a GLP program 𝑀 and agents 𝑃 ⊂ Π, the maGLP
transactions over asynchronous resolvents over 𝑀 are the following.
24                                                                                 Idit Keidar and Ehud Shapiro


Reduce 𝑝. 𝐴 ∈ 𝐺 𝑝 is a unit goal, and 𝐶 ∈ 𝑀 is the first clause for which the reduction of 𝐴 with 𝐶
(Definition 4.6) succeeds, with result (𝐵, 𝜎),
                                           ˆ 𝐶 renamed into V̂𝑝 .
                     𝐴 ∈ 𝐺𝑝       𝐶 the first clause reducing 𝐴, with result (𝐵, 𝜎)
                                                                                 ˆ
                               (𝐺 𝑝 , 𝜎𝑝 ) → ((𝐺 𝑝 \ {𝐴} ∪ 𝐵)𝜎,
                                                              ˆ 𝜎𝑝 ◦ 𝜎?)
                                                                      ˆ
Communicate 𝑝 to 𝑞. Participants 𝑝, 𝑞 ∈ 𝑃, possibly 𝑝 = 𝑞: {𝑋 ? := 𝑇 } ∈ 𝜎𝑝 and 𝑋 ? occurs in 𝐺𝑞 .
                                    {𝑋 ? := 𝑇 } ∈ 𝜎𝑝     𝑋 ? occurs in 𝐺𝑞
                  (𝐺 𝑝 , 𝜎𝑝 ), (𝐺𝑞 , 𝜎𝑞 ) → (𝐺 𝑝 , 𝜎𝑝 \ {𝑋 ? := 𝑇 }), (𝐺𝑞 {𝑋 ? := 𝑇 }, 𝜎𝑞 )
Where 𝑝 = 𝑞 the transaction is unary: (𝐺 𝑝 , 𝜎𝑝 ) → (𝐺 𝑝 {𝑋 ? := 𝑇 }, 𝜎𝑝 \ {𝑋 ? := 𝑇 }). Communicate
removes the assignment it communicates [53].
Cold-call 𝑝 to 𝑞. Participants 𝑝 ≠ 𝑞 ∈ 𝑃: the network output stream in (𝐺 𝑝 , 𝜎𝑝 ) has a new message
msg(𝑞, 𝑋 ); (𝐺 𝑝′ , 𝜎𝑝′ ) is the result of advancing the network output stream and (𝐺𝑞′ , 𝜎𝑞′ ) the result of
adding msg(𝑞, 𝑋 ) to the network input stream.
                         the network output stream of 𝑝 has a new msg(𝑞, 𝑋 )
                              (𝐺 𝑝 , 𝜎𝑝 ), (𝐺𝑞 , 𝜎𝑞 ) → (𝐺 𝑝′ , 𝜎𝑝′ ), (𝐺𝑞′ , 𝜎𝑞′ )

   Definition 4.11 (maGLP Transition System [53]). Given a GLP program 𝑀 and agents 𝑃 ⊂ Π,
the maGLP transition system over 𝑃 and 𝑀 is the transactions-based multiagent transition system
(Definition 2.10) over 𝑃, local states being asynchronous resolvents, initial local state ({𝑎 0 }, ∅) with
𝑎 0 the goal agent(ch(_?,_),ch(_?,_)), its anonymous variables at 𝑝 of V̂𝑝 , the transactions
of Definition 4.10, and the equivalence ∼ relating 𝑡 1 ∼ 𝑡 2 iff both are Reduce transactions at the
same agent 𝑝 reducing the same goal (by identity, Definition 4.8) with the same clause, both are
Communicate transactions from the same 𝑝 to the same 𝑞 applying the counterpart of the same
writer assignment to the same goal, or both are Cold-call transactions from the same 𝑝 to the same
𝑞 delivering the same message.

  Reduce differs from LP in the use of a writer mgu instead of a regular mgu, and in the choice of
the first applicable clause instead of any clause; Communicate communicates an assignment from its
writer to its paired reader — within one agent, or across the two agents holding the pair’s two sides;
Cold-call transfers a term with its variables through the network streams, creating paired variables
between previously-disconnected agents [53]. Every participant of each transaction changes state,
so the 𝑃-closures of distinct transactions are disjoint and ∼↑𝑃 is an equivalence (Definition 2.9).
Implementation-wise, if a goal 𝐴 cannot be reduced now, but there is a readers substitution under
which it can, the goal suspends on these readers and is rescheduled once any of them is assigned.

   Definition 4.12 (Proper Run [53]). A maGLP run is proper if, at each agent 𝑝, a variable that occurs
in 𝐺 𝑝 after a transition but not before it also does not occur in 𝐺 𝑝 at any earlier configuration.

  Definition 4.13 (cGLP [53]). Given a GLP program 𝑀, cGLP(𝑀) is the maGLP transition system
over a singleton set of agents and 𝑀.

  Cold-call requires two agents, so cGLP has Reduce and Communicate only; it is the single-agent
semantics.

  Proposition 4.14 (Monotonicity [53]). In any proper run of cGLP(𝑀), if unit goal 𝐴 can reduce
with clause 𝐶 at step 𝑖, then either an instance of 𝐴 has been reduced by step 𝑗 > 𝑖, or an instance of 𝐴
can still reduce with 𝐶 at step 𝑗.
Volition Elicitation                                                                                     25


5     VOLITION-GUARDED GLP
Here we define volition-guarded GLP (vGLP), the extension of GLP (Section 4) with volitional
procedures: a procedure may put a question to the person. Its goal receives an interactive variable
as an added argument, which the program and the person write together, each the positions its
type gives it, and a clause that reads the person’s part reduces only after the person has answered.
vGLP is typed as GLP is: the declaration of a volitional procedure carries its interactive type, and
widget declarations say how interactive types are displayed (Section 6).

5.1    Volitional Procedures
   Definition 5.1 (Volitional Procedure, Interactive Type). A volitional procedure 𝑝 of arity 𝑛 is declared
procedure (𝑇 )*𝑝 (𝑇1, . . . ,𝑇𝑛 )., where 𝑇1, . . . ,𝑇𝑛 are the types of its arguments and 𝑇 , in writer
or reader mode as an argument type is, is its interactive type. An interactive type is built from
constants, String, Integer, Real, Key, Constant, Module, named types so built, tuples and unions,
and Stream or List as the whole type or as an argument of a tuple, never under another Stream
or List, and a Stream only at a position the person writes; a named type in it is recursive only if it
has no mode inversion [59], and it has no type parameter.
  A speech act forwarded with its provenance is signed again at each forward [16], so its type is
recursive, and a request to forward it carries it. With no mode inversion, a recursive type is written
whole by the program or whole by the person.
    Definition 5.2 (Clause of a Volitional Procedure, Interactive Term). A clause of a volitional procedure
𝑝 with interactive type 𝑇 has the form (𝐴)*𝑝 (𝑆 1, . . . , 𝑆𝑛 ) :- 𝐺 | 𝐵, where the interactive term 𝐴 is
a term of type 𝑇 , possibly a variable but not the anonymous variable, and it is the guarded clause
𝑝 (𝑆 1, . . . , 𝑆𝑛 , 𝐴) :- 𝐺 | 𝐵 (Definition 4.2), of arity 𝑛+1. A clause of a volitional procedure reads an
argument at an input position, by a non-variable term at that position in its head or by a guard on
it, only if every later clause of the procedure reads it too, and every call of a volitional procedure,
in a clause body or in the initial goal, holds a writer at each output position.
  Definition 5.3 (Ordinary Clause, vGLP Program). A clause is ordinary if it is not a clause of
a volitional procedure. A vGLP program is a finite sequence of ordinary clauses and clauses of
volitional procedures, each satisfying SRSW, a clause of a volitional procedure satisfying it as a
guarded clause of arity 𝑛+1; the clauses for the same predicate form a procedure. The set of goals
Ĝ(𝑀) of a vGLP program 𝑀 includes all goals over V̂ and the vocabulary of 𝑀 that satisfy SO.
   The last argument of the guarded clause is matched against the goal’s end of its interactive
variable, and a goal of a volitional procedure of arity 𝑛 is 𝑛-ary until it is asked and of arity 𝑛+1
after (Definition 5.8).
   Here are vGLP fragments of the currency agent of the repository (Section 7), written by hand
in this syntax, in which ... elides arguments the clause carries through unchanged. The agent is
a volitional procedure whose question is the stream of the person’s requests, of a union type, an
alternative per request, served by an ordinary procedure:
Friend ::= Key.
Amount ::= Integer.
Request ::= mint(Amount) ; pay(Friend, Amount) ; ... .
procedure (Stream(Request)?)*agent(...).

%% Ask the person for their requests, once, and serve them.
(Reqs)*agent(Id, ..., Holdings, Serial) :-
26                                                                           Idit Keidar and Ehud Shapiro


     serve(Reqs?, Id?, ..., Holdings?, Serial?).

%% Pay_{p,q}(k), guarded by p: k q-coins to q.
serve([pay(Friend, Amount)|Reqs], Id, ..., Holdings,
      Serial) :-
    ground(Id?), ground(Friend?), integer(Amount?),
    Amount? >= 1 |
    select_coins(Friend?, Amount?, Holdings?, Status,
                 Give, Rest),
    do_pay(Status?, Id?, Friend?, Amount?, Give?, Rest?,
           Reqs?, ..., Serial?).
%% A peer proposes a swap: spawn a responder, which asks
%% the person, and go on without waiting for a request.
on_act(swap(Want, Offered, Resp?), From, Id, Reqs,
       UserIn, ...) :-
    ground(Want?), ground(Offered?) |
    respond_swap(offer(From?, Want?, Offered?), Resp, Ans),
    merge(Ans?, UserIn?, UserIn1),
    serve(Reqs?, Id?, UserIn1?, ...).

%% yes accepts, no declines; the agent hears of either
%% on its own input.
decide_swap(yes, From, Want, Offered, Resp?, Ans?) :-
    accept_answer(From?, Want?, Offered?, Resp, Ans).
decide_swap(no, From, _, Offered, Resp?, Ans?) :-
    decline_answer(From?, Offered?, Resp, Ans).
The agent asks its question once and passes the stream to serve, ordinary GLP; the person answers
one request at a time, and the clause of that request’s alternative reads it at the head of the stream:
the pay clause waits for a pay request. The clause of an arriving swap proposal reads no request, so
it reduces without waiting and passes the stream on. The responder’s question, respond_swap of
Section 1, carries output as well as input: its interactive type is in writer mode, so its clause does
not wait but writes the approval question at once, with the proposal at the position Offer for the
person to see, and hands the person the writer at the position YesNo?; the clauses of decide_swap
each wait for the person’s answer, and a decline is an answer, no, taken by its clause. A clause whose
interactive term is a variable reduces without waiting and passes the reader on, so the question
stays open until the person answers it:
Status ::= sent.
Line ::= line(String, Status?).
Msg ::= msg(Peer, String).
procedure (Stream(Line)?)*chat(Peer?, Stream(Msg)).
(Ls)*chat(Peer, Out?) :- ground(Peer?) |
    send_all(Peer?, Ls?, Out).

send_all(Peer, [line(Text, sent)|Ls], [msg(Peer?, Text?)|Out?]) :-
    ground(Peer?) | send_all(Peer?, Ls?, Out).
send_all(_, [], []).
Volition Elicitation                                                                                             27


The chat input is one question whose answer is a stream, which the person gives one line at a time,
and send_all is ordinary GLP, calling itself on the tail of the stream after each line. Each line has a
position the program writes, its status: the person’s answer hands the program its writer (Answer,
Definition 5.7), and send_all writes sent into it, by head unification, as it puts the message on the
network, so the line the person typed shows as pending until it is sent and as sent after. A question
whose type has no position the program writes is bare, as the agent’s request stream is.

5.2    Operational Semantics
Here we define vmaGLP, the operational semantics of vGLP, which extends maGLP (Definition 4.11)
with the person of each agent: an agent’s state adds the variables its person holds, and three
transactions, each at one agent, add the person’s part. What vGLP adds is thus local; the semantics
is multiagent only as maGLP is, by Communicate and Cold-call, and Communicate delivers the
program’s output to the person as it delivers an assignment to a goal.
   Definition 5.4 (vmaGLP Agent State). Given a vGLP program 𝑀, an agent state is a triple
(𝐻𝑝 , 𝐺 𝑝 , 𝜎𝑝 ) of a finite set 𝐻𝑝 of typed variables held by the agent’s person and of assignments shown
to them and an asynchronous resolvent (𝐺 𝑝 , 𝜎𝑝 ) over 𝑀 (Definition 4.11). A shown assignment is a
record of what the person was shown, and its variables are not occurrences.
   Definition 5.5 (Reduce and Communicate in vmaGLP). Reduce applies to a unit goal that is not
an 𝑛-ary goal of a volitional procedure, and a clause of a volitional procedure is taken as its
guarded clause (Definition 5.2). Communicate delivers an assignment {𝑋 ? := 𝑇 } ∈ 𝜎𝑝 to a reader
𝑋 ? ∈ 𝐻𝑞 once 𝑇ˆ , the term 𝑇 with each reader replaced, repeatedly, by the term assigned to it in the
configuration, has no reader, and then delivers 𝑇ˆ : it removes 𝑋 ? from 𝐻𝑞 and adds the variables
of 𝑇ˆ , each with the type of its position in 𝑇ˆ , and the assignment, shown, the assignments used in
forming 𝑇ˆ being communicated with it; Reduce, Cold-call and every other Communicate leave the
held variables unchanged.
    Definition 5.6 (Ask). The transactions on the person’s variables at an agent 𝑝 are Ask of this
definition and Answer of the next. Ask 𝑝: 𝐴 is an 𝑛-ary goal of a volitional procedure 𝑞 with
interactive type 𝑇 , 𝑋 a writer of V𝑝 occurring nowhere in the configuration, the interactive variable
of the asked goal, of type 𝑇 ′ where 𝑇 is 𝑇 ′ or 𝑇 ′ ?; the goal takes its end of 𝑋 and the person the
other: (𝐴′, 𝑌 ) = (𝑞(𝑆 1, . . . , 𝑆𝑛 , 𝑋 ?), 𝑋 ) where 𝑇 is in reader mode, and (𝑞(𝑆 1, . . . , 𝑆𝑛 , 𝑋 ), 𝑋 ?) where
it is in writer mode. The asked goal keeps the identifier of 𝐴.
                         𝐴 = 𝑞(𝑆 1, . . . , 𝑆𝑛 ) ∈ 𝐺 𝑝 𝑋 fresh    (𝐴′, 𝑌 ) as above
                           (𝐻𝑝 , 𝐺 𝑝 , 𝜎𝑝 ) → (𝐻𝑝 ∪ {𝑌 }, 𝐺 𝑝 \ {𝐴} ∪ {𝐴′ }, 𝜎𝑝 )
   Definition 5.7 (Answer). Answer 𝑝: 𝑋 ∈ 𝐻𝑝 is a writer whose reader 𝑋 ? occurs in the configuration,
and 𝑡 a term of the type of 𝑋 , ground but for variables of V̂𝑝 occurring nowhere in the configuration:
a reader 𝑌𝑖 ? at each position the person writes of type Stream(𝑇 ′ ) or List(𝑇 ′ ), unless the person
ends the list there with [], and a writer 𝑊 𝑗 at each outermost position the program writes; where
𝑋 itself is of such a type, 𝑡 is [𝑒|𝑌1 ?] with 𝑒 such a term of type 𝑇 ′ , or [] for a list. The person
holds 𝑌𝑖 and 𝑊 𝑗 ?, each with the type of its position in 𝑡.
                                          𝑋 ∈ 𝐻𝑝           𝑡 as above
             (𝐻𝑝 , 𝐺 𝑝 , 𝜎𝑝 ) → (𝐻𝑝 \ {𝑋 } ∪ {𝑌1, . . . , 𝑌𝑘 ,𝑊1 ?, . . . ,𝑊𝑚 ?}, 𝐺 𝑝 , 𝜎𝑝 ∪ {𝑋 ? := 𝑡 })
  Definition 5.8 (vmaGLP Transition System). Given a vGLP program 𝑀 and agents 𝑃 ⊂ Π, the
vmaGLP transition system over 𝑃 and 𝑀 is the transactions-based multiagent transition system
(Definition 2.10) over 𝑃, local states being agent states, initial local state (∅, {𝑎 0 }, ∅) with 𝑎 0 as
28                                                                             Idit Keidar and Ehud Shapiro


in Definition 4.11, the transactions being the Reduce, Communicate and Cold-call transactions
of maGLP as Definition 5.5 adapts them and the transactions on the person’s variables, Ask and
Answer (Definitions 5.6 and 5.7), read as those of Definition 4.10, and the equivalence ∼ below. ∼ is
the partial equivalence whose domain is the Ask, Reduce, Communicate and Cold-call transactions,
relating two Reduce, Communicate or Cold-call transactions as maGLP does, the person counting
as a goal, and 𝑡 1 ∼ 𝑡 2 iff both are Ask transactions at the same agent 𝑝 asking the same goal (by
identity, Definition 4.8).
   Every participant of each transaction changes state, so the 𝑃-closures of distinct transactions are
disjoint and ∼↑𝑃 is an equivalence (Definition 2.9). Ask is the pre-reduction of a goal of a volitional
procedure. Before the goal can reduce, it receives its end of its interactive variable as its added
argument: the reader where the person writes the variable, and the writer where the program does.
The person holds the other end. The semantics fixes no moment for Ask other than before the
goal’s reduction; the implementation asks at the goal’s first reduction attempt (Section 6).
   Answer is the person’s act: the program reads an answer only through its reader, and the person
performs every answer. Answer lies outside the domain of the equivalence and carries no liveness
obligation, since the machine cannot compel the person. An answer is an assignment and cannot
be withdrawn; the person edits it in its construct before submitting it (Section 6).
   An interactive type carries output to the person as well as input from the person. Its moded
type [59] says which positions the program writes and which the person writes, and each side,
writing a position, hands the other the variables of the positions below it that the other writes.
Communicate delivers the program’s output to the person whole, every position the program
writes in it written, with the writers the person answers, and keeps it in 𝐻𝑞 as shown. So the person
holds the writer of an input only with the whole output around it, and sees what they are asked
about before answering (Section 6). An answer is ground at the positions the person writes, but
for a stream or a list. An answer of type Stream(𝑇 ) is given one element at a time, each element
leaving the person the writer of the stream’s tail as a new question, and an answer of type List(𝑇 )
likewise until the person ends it. A stream or list inside an answer is left to the person as a new
question, answered element by element.
  Proposition 5.9 (Conservativity). For a GLP program 𝑀, the runs of the vmaGLP transition
system over 𝑃 and 𝑀, with (∅, 𝐺𝑝 , 𝜎𝑝 ) identified with (𝐺 𝑝 , 𝜎𝑝 ), are the runs of the maGLP transition
system over 𝑃 and 𝑀, and the same runs are correct in both.
  Proof. 𝑀 has no volitional procedure, so in a run no goal is asked and the person holds no
variable: Ask and Answer have no instances and every 𝐻𝑝 is ∅, so no reader is held. Reduce,
Communicate, and Cold-call are those of maGLP with 𝐻𝑝 unchanged, and ∼ is its equivalence, total
on them.                                                                                      □

  Definition 5.10 (Proper Run, adapted from [53]). A vmaGLP run is proper if, at each agent 𝑝, a
variable that occurs in 𝐺 𝑝 after a transition but not before it also does not occur in 𝐺 𝑝 or in 𝐻𝑝 at
any earlier configuration, and the clause each Reduce renames is renamed apart from every agent’s
goals, held variables and readers substitution, in the configuration it reduces and in every earlier
one.
  Definition 5.11 (vcGLP). Given a vGLP program 𝑀, vcGLP(𝑀) is the vmaGLP transition system
over a singleton set of agents and 𝑀.
  Cold-call requires two agents, so vcGLP has Reduce, Communicate, Ask and Answer only; it is
the single-agent semantics.
Volition Elicitation                                                                                   29


   Proposition 5.12 (Monotonicity in vcGLP). In any proper run of vcGLP(𝑀), if unit goal 𝐴
can reduce with clause 𝐶 at step 𝑖, then either an instance of 𝐴 has been reduced by step 𝑗 > 𝑖, or an
instance of 𝐴 can still reduce with 𝐶 at step 𝑗.
  Proof. In vcGLP a goal of a volitional procedure reduces only once asked, as a GLP goal with
the guarded clauses of its procedure, and an answer reaches it as a communicated assignment, so
Proposition 4.14 holds verbatim.                                                              □
   Definition 5.13 (Question, Open, Closed, Volition). In a configuration of the vmaGLP transition
system, a writer 𝑋 ∈ 𝐻𝑝 is a question of 𝑝’s person: an interactive variable the person writes,
introduced by an Ask, a writer inside the program’s output delivered to the person, or the writer of
a stream or list that an Answer leaves to the person, the tail of the stream or list answered or one
inside the answer; it is open if its reader 𝑋 ? occurs in the configuration, and closed otherwise. A
volition of 𝑝’s person is an Answer transition of 𝑝: the person’s answer to a question.
    An open question is what the machine puts to its person: what is your answer? A question is
answered by Answer, which takes it from the person’s variables; it closes when its reader no longer
occurs, the program having dropped the reader by the anonymous variable at an input position [53];
in writer mode the program so closes a question inside its output and, where the type provides for
it, writes more of the output — a status that the widget reads, say. A closed question asks nothing
more of the person, and cannot be answered (Definition 5.7). How questions reach the screen and
answers return is the interface’s task (Section 6).

5.3    Volitional Soundness
The machine never answers for the person: a question is assigned by its person’s answer and by no
transition of the machine.
   Theorem 5.14 (Volitional Soundness). Let 𝑐 1 → 𝑐 2 → · · · be a proper safe run of the vmaGLP
transition system over 𝑃 and 𝑀, 𝑝 ∈ 𝑃, and 𝑋 a question of 𝑝’s person in 𝑐 𝑖 . Then the writer 𝑋 occurs
in 𝑐 𝑖 in 𝐻𝑝 and nowhere else, and a transition 𝑐 𝑖 → 𝑐 𝑖+1 that adds an assignment 𝑋 ? := 𝑡 to a readers
substitution is the Answer of 𝑝 to 𝑋 .
  The proof is in Appendix B. Every reduction that reads an answer therefore reads a term the
person gave. The theorem says who gives an answer. The person sees a whole proposal before
answering it, since a question reaches its person with its output written (Definition 5.5). Once
answered, a goal is a GLP goal, and a correct run reduces it (Proposition 5.15).
  Proposition 5.15 (Answered Goals Reduce). Let 𝑟 be a proper correct run of vcGLP(𝑀) and
𝐴 an asked goal in it whose question the person answers with a term on which some clause of 𝐴’s
procedure reduces. Then an instance of 𝐴 is reduced in 𝑟 .
    Proof. The answer is an assignment 𝑋 ? := 𝑡 in the readers substitution with 𝑋 ? in 𝐴; the
Communicate of it to 𝐴 is in the domain of the equivalence and enabled until taken, so 𝑟 takes it,
and 𝐴 then holds 𝑡. From there 𝐴 is a GLP goal with the guarded clauses of its procedure, and a
clause reduces it on 𝑡; by Monotonicity (Proposition 5.12) every clause that succeeds on 𝐴 goes on
succeeding until an instance of 𝐴 is reduced, so the first clause that succeeds on 𝐴, which is the
clause of its Reduce, changes only to an earlier one, finitely often; from some configuration on it is
one clause, the Reduce class of 𝐴 with that clause is enabled in every configuration, and 𝑟 takes
it.                                                                                                 □
   The person’s requests are served in the same way: in the agent of Section 5, a request at the head
of the stream is read by the clause of its alternative, which comes before the message clause, so the
30                                                                                    Idit Keidar and Ehud Shapiro


next reduction of serve takes it; a clause placed before the request clauses that reduces without
reading the stream could put a request off while its trigger keeps arriving, and the agent goals of
Section 5.4 place none, reading every trigger from one fair merge.

5.4   Compiling the Canonical CVA Platform into vGLP
A CVA platform specifies a set of app transactions (Definition 3.4), possibly infinite and written in
no syntax, and no program implements every such set. Here we compile into vGLP the canonical
platform of Section 3.2 of every effective local specification, with a computable introduction rule or
none (Definition 3.18), one goal per agent, and prove the compilation correct.
   Definition 5.16 (Effective Local Specification). A local volition-guarded specification is effective
if its machine states are finite terms, its guarded parts, which enter the approval questions, are
terms of an interactive type (Definition 5.1), each with a request name, a term of an interactive
type in which no Stream or List occurs, and its unguarded parts are finite terms; the parts of a
class are finitely many and computable from any one of them, 𝑄 ( [𝑡]) from any part of [𝑡], and

   The conditions and effects of the canonical platform of an effective specification are computable
and terminate: they test and apply the specification’s functions and the guard sets of its parts. A
name of a transaction is of an interactive type: a guarded part is a term of one, and an attempt of
an unguarded part, which may hold a list, as a delivery holds its members, is named by its initiator
and number.
Triggers. The compiled agent takes each app transaction of the canonical platform on a trigger:
Request on its person’s answer propose(𝑁 ) to a request question, 𝑁 the request name of a
guarded part, on which it takes, among the Request of {[𝑡]} to itself for each class [𝑡] of a guarded
part with no opener named 𝑁 that it proposes and is ready for, and the Request of 𝜌 𝑁 to the opener
of the parts with an opener named 𝑁 , the one it has taken least recently; Stand and Withdraw
on its person’s request of the act a pragma declares as making or withdrawing a standing request
(Appendix B, the contract’s words); Start, Tally, Yield, Outcome and Readied on the arriving
message; Answer on its person’s answer to the approval question that Start raises, one per attempt,
asked by a goal that shows the attempt and takes yes or no; and Propose, Vote, Commit, Wound,
Ready and Introduce peers, its own transactions, one at a time, each on an own trigger that the
Volition Elicitation                                                                                 31


agent writes on a stream merged fairly with its other triggers, when it starts, after another trigger
and after each own transaction it takes, until none holds. So the person answers two kinds of
question: request questions, each taking one request, and approval questions; the agent’s own key
and the persons it knows are in the view it writes. A request is taken when it is read, or held while
the agent is not free for its classes and taken when it is, or dropped, with a notice, where the agent
can carry out no part it names; once taken it is an approval, as an answer yes is, which stands until
the transaction is taken or refused, and the person sees their pending approvals in the view; the
agent proposes, and proposes again an attempt that yields, with no further question to its person. A
machine that cannot carry out a transaction its person approved refuses it; when a refusal ends an
approval, the person who made it is told who refused the transaction and why, declined or unable.
The else-branch of Answer is taken on the person’s answer no, where CVA takes it on the absence
of the will, so an approval question waits for its person.

The compilation. Given an effective local volition-guarded specification and its canonical platform
Θ, the compilation ⌈Θ⌉ is the following vGLP program, standard stream techniques used without
comment [53]. The agent calls the agent goal, which, where the specification has a guarded part, asks
its person for requests by request questions: each is a goal of a volitional procedure whose question,
of the request type Request?, takes one request, of a type with the alternative propose(𝑁 ) for
the request name 𝑁 of each guarded part; the agent goal asks one when it starts and the next when
it reads the answer to the last. On the program’s output to its person (Section 6.3) the agent goal
writes its view view(𝑘, 𝑠, 𝐾, 𝐴, 𝑇 ), its own key 𝑘, the machine state 𝑠 of its app state, the
persons 𝐾 it knows, the approvals 𝐴 its person made that are pending, each by its request name,
a request’s own and an answer’s that of its attempt’s part, and the tabs 𝑇 the contract’s pragmas
name, each its name and the predicate it lists, when it starts and whenever any of the first four
changes; and, when an approval its person made, by a request or by an answer yes, ends by a
refusal, a notice refused(𝑁 , 𝑞, 𝑑): 𝑁 the request name of the part of the refused attempt, 𝑞 its
refuser and 𝑑 the reason, from the abort outcome, or from the vote where the agent’s own vote
no refused it. The compilation defines Output as the type of the views and the notices, and prints
the platform’s three widget declarations (Definition 6.5, Section 6.2): for the request type, for the
approval type and for Stream(Output).
    The agent goal is an ordinary procedure holding the agent state — the app state, initially
(𝑠 0, 0, ⊥, ∅); a count of the own transactions it has taken, initially 0; the peers hello has been
sent to and received from; the starts it holds waiting; the requests it holds; the requests it has made,
in the order it last made them; a writer for each open approval question, by which it closes it; the
count at which each class of its own transactions began to wait; and the writer of its stream of
own triggers — and reading one input stream, the fair merge (Example 4.3) of the answers to its
request questions, the messages arriving on the network, the answers to its approval questions
and its own triggers; where the contract requires the day, it reads the date from its phone as it
computes a condition.
    The agent goal has one clause per kind of trigger, which reads the trigger at the head of the
stream, computes in its body the condition of the transaction the trigger puts up, on the state it
was called with, and calls a dispatch procedure with its value, each of whose clauses calls the agent
goal again. A dispatch clause that takes a transaction carries out its effect: the new app state; the
messages it sends, appended to the network output stream; for a Start that records a question,
an approval question; and the closing of the open approval question of an attempt the transaction
closes; and, where the machine state changes, it writes the new view on the output. An approval
question is asked by a volitional procedure whose question shows the attempt and takes yes or
no; the procedure also takes the reader of the writer by which the agent goal closes it, and its one
32                                                                              Idit Keidar and Ehud Shapiro


clause hands that reader and the reader of the answer to an ordinary goal, which passes the answer
on where it comes first and, where that reader is assigned first, drops the answer’s reader, which
closes the question (Section 5).
    On the answer propose(𝑁 ) to a request question the dispatch takes, among the Request of
{[𝑡]} to itself for each class [𝑡] of a guarded part with no opener named 𝑁 that the agent proposes
and is ready for, where it is free for [𝑡] and no such request is pending at it, and the Request
of 𝜌 𝑁 to the opener of the guarded parts with an opener named 𝑁 , where the agent is free for
every class of 𝜌 𝑁 , guards one of those parts it is ready for, and 𝜌 𝑁 is not pending at it, the one it
has taken least recently, where there is one; where the request is of an act a pragma declares as
making a standing request, the dispatch takes also the Stand of that standing request, and where
of an act declared as withdrawing it, the Withdraw; where the agent is not free for a class of such
a Request or Stand, it holds the request and puts it up again after every dispatch; and it drops
the request where the agent is free and none is enabled, with a notice refused(𝑁 , 𝑝, unable)
where the agent is ready for no part named 𝑁 that it guards or opens; the message of the Request
carries 𝑁 for 𝜌 𝑁 . On a message request(𝜌, 𝑘) it holds the request until it deletes it, at the refusal of
an attempt naming it or at a commit of its class. On a message start(𝛼) it takes Start where the
start does not wait (Definition 3.11) or 𝛼 is closed at the agent, and otherwise holds it waiting; on
vote(𝛼, 𝑦) it takes Tally, on yield(𝛼) Yield, on outcome(𝛼, 𝑧) Outcome, and on ready(𝛼) Readied.
A vote yes carries the readings of the agent’s machine state, and the Tally of an attempt’s last yes
chooses, among the readings recorded with the votes and its own, the first in a fixed order that
meet the condition of the attempt’s part. On the answer to the approval question of 𝛼 it takes the
then-branch of Answer on yes and its else-branch on no, where 𝛼 is a question pending at the
agent and unanswered, and drops the answer otherwise.
    The agent goal takes its own transactions one at a time, one on each own trigger it reads. It
writes an own trigger when it starts, after each other trigger it reads unless an own trigger it has
written is still unread, and after each own transaction it takes, so that it reads its other triggers
between its own transactions and writes none once none holds. On an own trigger it puts up the
Propose of each unguarded part the agent opens, and of each guarded part with no opener that
it proposes of the class of an approval pending at it, named by that approval where the approval
is a request, where its machine state is in the part’s domain, or, where it is in the domain of no
such part, of one of them, which it refuses at once; for each request it holds, the Propose of each
guarded part named by the request’s name that it opens and whose domain holds its machine
state, naming that request, or, where there is none, of one such part, which it refuses at once; the
Vote on each answered question pending at it, the Commit of each attempt it initiated with a yes
of every other participant recorded, and the Wound of each start it holds waiting, or answered
question, blocked by an attempt over which it has priority, the Ready of each attempt it refused as
unable whose part’s domain now holds its machine state, and of each attempt it voted yes on that
its initiator refused on its condition, once it has committed an attempt since, and the Introduce
peers of each pair the introduction rule gives it at its machine state; the dispatch takes, among
those whose condition holds, one of the class that has waited longest — since it was last taken or,
never taken, since its condition was first found holding — ties broken in a fixed order, and counts
it; where none holds, it takes none. After every dispatch that changes the app state or consumes a
start the agent goal puts up again every request of its person it holds, and then the starts it holds,
so that a request held while the agent was busy is an approval before a start of its class can ask its
person again.
    The compilation is given, with the specification, its acquaintance parts: guarded parts with two
guards and no opener whose guards need not know each other, for a contract the parts of an
acquaintance schema, one with two roles, both guarding, that requires at neither role an atom
Volition Elicitation                                                                                33


naming the other. GKK’s acquaint is one, by which two persons come to know each other (Section 7);
CSSN’s enter is another, by which a parent, holding both phones, sets up their child’s phone, the
bootstrap of parenting [54]. The request of an acquaintance part is also a cold call. On the request
propose(𝑁 ), 𝑁 the request name of an acquaintance part with a peer 𝑄, the agent goal, besides
what it does on any request, sends 𝑄 hello carrying 𝑁 and records 𝑄 as sent to, unless 𝑄 is known
or already sent to; the person who requests is the part’s proposer, and the person called is its other
guard. On an arriving hello from 𝑄 it records 𝑄 as received from and, unless it has sent 𝑄 a hello,
asks its person the approval question of the clause of 𝑁 , with its pragma, by a goal that shows 𝑄
and takes yes or no: the person’s yes is their will for the acquaintance with 𝑄, which the agent
goal holds, and by which it answers yes, without asking again, the approval question that the start
of the proposer’s attempt raises; on their no it does nothing. On reading an introduction intro(𝑄)
it sends 𝑄 ihello, unless 𝑄 is known or it has sent one, and an arriving ihello it records likewise.
A peer is known at the agent when hello, or ihello, has been sent to it and received from it, and
a condition’s test that a peer is known is of these; when a peer becomes known, the agent goal
writes the new view on the output.
   The reduction of a dispatch clause that takes a transaction, or the else-branch of Answer, is that
transaction. Discover is the sending of the second of the two hellos, and Communicate is the agent
goal’s reading of a message the network streams carried to it. Every other reduction is a stutter.
   Definition 5.17 (Platform Mapping). Given an effective local volition-guarded specification, its
canonical platform Θ, 𝑃 ⊂ Π and a configuration of the vmaGLP transition system over 𝑃 and ⌈Θ⌉,
the platform mapping 𝜎 yields the configuration of P with, at each agent 𝑝: known𝑝 the 𝑞 ∈ 𝑃 such
that 𝑝 and 𝑞 have each sent the other hello, or each sent the other ihello; 𝑜 𝑝 the app messages
on 𝑝’s network output stream not yet read by their recipient’s agent goal; 𝑖 𝑝 the app messages 𝑝’s
agent goal has read and not yet dispatched, the starts it holds waiting, the requests it holds, and the
introductions it has read of peers with which it has not exchanged ihello; 𝑎𝑝 the app state the
agent goal holds or, between the reduction of a dispatch clause and the agent goal’s next call, the
one its effect yields.
   Definition 5.18 (Platform Mapping: Volitional State). At each agent 𝑝, the platform mapping 𝜎
yields the volitional state holding 𝑝’s approval class on a class [𝑡] while the agent goal has read
a request propose(𝑁 ) that puts up the Request of {[𝑡]}, 𝑁 naming a guarded part of [𝑡] that 𝑝
guards, and has not yet taken or dropped it, or reads the answer yes to the approval question of an
attempt of [𝑡] — from its reading until its dispatch — the condition of the Answer, but for its test
of known peers, holding at 𝑝’s machine state; holding 𝑝’s request class on 𝜌 𝑁 while the agent goal
has read a request propose(𝑁 ) that puts up the Request of 𝜌 𝑁 , of two or more classes, and has
not yet taken or dropped it, and its standing class, or its withdrawing class, on a standing request 𝜌
while it has read a request that puts up the Stand, or the Withdraw, of 𝜌 and has not yet taken or
dropped it; holding 𝑝’s approval class on [𝑡] also while the agent goal holds its person’s yes to a
hello of an acquaintance part of [𝑡] and has not yet answered the approval question of an attempt
of [𝑡] by it; and the class of Discover(𝑝, 𝑞), 𝑞 ∈ 𝑃, while a request of 𝑝’s person of an acquaintance
part with 𝑞 is pending at 𝑝, or their yes to 𝑞’s hello is held, and 𝑞 ∉ known𝑝 .

  Theorem 5.19 (Compilation of the Canonical Platform). For every effective local volition-
guarded specification and 𝑃 ⊂ Π, the platform mapping is a correct implementation of P, the transition
system of its canonical platform with a computable introduction rule or none over 𝑃 under its fairness
condition (Definition 3.22), by the vmaGLP transition system over 𝑃 and ⌈Θ⌉, under the fairness
condition that a run is proper, no approval question stays open in it, and its image under the platform
mapping is connected, unobstructed, steady and resolute.
34                                                                              Idit Keidar and Ehud Shapiro


   The proof is in Appendix B. The condition on the person concerns the approval questions: the
request question need not be answered, since a request not made wills nothing, but an approval
question left open keeps Answer from being taken by either branch; an approval question whose
attempt closes first is closed by the program. The condition on the run is that of Theorem 3.24 but
for strong fairness, which the compilation secures (Section 6.4). Completeness is not claimed: the
compilation takes the agent’s own transactions as soon as their conditions hold, in a fixed order,
which P does not require.
Grassroots. The canonical platform is volitionally grassroots (Proposition 3.6). The protocol of its
compilation assigns to each 𝑃 ⊂ Π the vmaGLP transition system over 𝑃 and ⌈Θ⌉, its local states
over 𝑃 being the agent states in which every agent identifier is of an agent of 𝑃, and its correct
runs being those that are correct and proper.
   Proposition 5.20 (Grassroots Compilation). For every effective local volition-guarded speci-
fication with acquaintance parts guarded by every two agents, and computable introduction rule or
none, the protocol of the compilation of its canonical platform is grassroots (Definition A.3).
  The proof is in Appendix B. vmaGLP has no guards, so the stronger property, volitionally
grassroots, does not apply to it.
Speech-act schemas. A contract written as speech-act schemas [16] is the layer above the trans-
actions in Figure 1. A compiler written in GLP takes such a contract, whose roles are party roles
or listed roles [16], checks that it is a local and effective specification, and prints the compilation
of its canonical platform in vGLP, in the form of this section. It does so in one pass: it produces
neither the volition-guarded transactions the contract specifies nor the canonical platform, and the
program it prints carries the schemas and computes the condition and effect of each transaction
from them as it runs. It compiles the social graph and the social network, GSG and GSN [16], and
the currencies, GC [49] (Section 7), and the test suite runs each compiled platform on GLP with
scripted persons. A vote unable of a compiled program carries the atoms the schema requires at
the voter that its state lacks, and those it forbids that its state has, and the notice shows them.
Speech-act schemas as local specifications. A speech-act schema is 𝜏 : 𝐻 ← 𝐵 guarded by
𝐺 [16]: its body 𝐵 is a finite multiset of literals and safe formulas, its head 𝐻 a finite multiset
of literals and quantified conjuncts, over atoms whose arguments are agent variables, speech-act
variables, name terms and the wildcard _, the first argument of an atom its holder, and 𝐺 is a set of
its agent variables, its guards. A safe formula is built from atoms and constraints by ∧, ∨ and ¬
and by the quantifiers ∃𝑥¯ (𝐴 ∧ 𝜑) and ∀¯  𝑥 (𝐴 ∧ 𝜑 → 𝜓 ), 𝐴 an atom, the quantifier’s anchor, among
whose arguments every variable of 𝑥¯ occurs; a quantified conjunct is ∀¯       𝑥 (𝐴 ∧ 𝜑 → 𝐿1 ∧ · · · ∧ 𝐿𝑚 )
with literals 𝐿 𝑗 ; and a name term ⟨𝜋⟩ · 𝑦1 · · · 𝑦𝑛 names the community of the person bound to the
agent variable 𝜋, its base, and the speech acts bound to 𝑦1, . . . , 𝑦𝑛 , as ⟨𝑎⟩ · 𝑛 names the group 𝑛 led
by 𝑎 [16]. An agent role is an agent variable that no quantifier binds and that holds an atom or is a
guard; a listed role is an agent variable bound by a quantifier whose anchor an agent role, its base,
holds, the variable at any of the anchor’s arguments, and that holds an atom, written 𝜋 [𝐴], 𝜋 its
base and the bound variables replaced by _ in 𝐴; a role is guarding if it is a guard, every person
in a listed guarding role being a guard; an agent role requires and forbids the atoms of 𝐵 it holds,
unnegated and negated, and adds, deletes and clears those of 𝐻 , and a listed role does the same by
the literals it holds in the conclusions of the conjuncts that bind it; the literals an agent role holds
in a conjunct of 𝐻 that binds no listed role are conditional, added or deleted at each instance of the
conjunct; a role that is not guarding and adds and deletes nothing is a reading role; and the variables
of a schema are its agent variables that are not roles and its speech-act variables that no quantifier
binds [16]. A schema-binding binds the roles and the variables of a schema: roles to people, distinct
Volition Elicitation                                                                                     35


agent roles to distinct persons but for two the clause names as possibly one person, which it may
bind to one person where neither adds or deletes an atom naming the other; and the variables to
people and to speech acts, a speech act being an utterance or a speech act, signed; a signed term
sgn(𝑦, 𝑖) in an added atom is the speech act bound to 𝑦, signed by the person in role 𝑖 [16]. A state is
a finite multiset of atoms, so a predicate of arity zero carries a count; a contract opens by declaring
its names, as “Alice, Bob and Carol are persons”; a listed role and the role whose person lists it,
and two roles bound to one person, are carried out together, the atoms at both required, forbidden,
deleted and added at that person, joined, with the greater multiplicity [16]; a predicate of arity one
that the contract declares reflexive holds at every person of that person; a wildcard in a negated
atom is universally quantified, the atom forbidding, in 𝐵, and clearing, in 𝐻 , every atom of the state
that it matches, as GSN’s unfriend ends every membership between the two; and no atom a schema
adds at a person is one it deletes there or one a cleared atom there matches [16]. Speech-act schemas
allow values [16]: an argument of an atom may be a value — a whole number, a real, a string, a
program, a GLP module as text, which the runtime recognises, or a constant the contract names,
a value or an utterance — or a value variable, whose type, a whole number or a real, the schema
writes with it, a multiplicity and a count being whole numbers; and an argument of an atom may
be the signer, the author or the item of a speech act, as GSC’s term form writes it; a schema may
carry a constraint, a decidable condition on the values its binding assigns, which is evaluated when
the schema is bound; a constraint, and a safe formula, is over the relations and functions the value
types provide — comparison and arithmetic, min and floor; the signer, the author and the item of a
speech act, its last signer, its first signer and its utterance [16]; and signed (𝑢, 𝑦) and unsigned (𝑢, 𝑦),
that the person bound to 𝑢 signed the speech act bound to 𝑦 or did not; and program(𝑢), that the
value 𝑢 is a program; and any other relation or function of a value type, which the owner of the
type gives as a typed GLP procedure that decides the relation or computes the function [59], and
which the platform the compiler prints calls — and over the count and the sum of the instances of
a quantifier, each instance counted as many times as its anchor’s multiplicity [16]; and an atom
it requires, adds or deletes may carry a multiplicity, a number or a value variable, for that many
copies of it, an atom required with a multiplicity 𝑘 and deleted with none having 𝑘 copies required
and one deleted. A variable that occurs only in atoms the schema requires or deletes at one role,
and in the constraint and the safe formulas, is local to that role, as the person 𝑑 where Bob’s role
requires that Bob point to someone with a label; and so is a variable the constraint determines
from the variables local to that role and the variables the schema-binding binds, as the count 𝑖 1 a
treasurer’s state holds after minting 𝑘 coins with 𝑖 held before, 𝑖 1 = 𝑖 + 𝑘. A variable is also local to
a role other than the base of the schema (below), and read there, where atoms the schema requires
at that role hold it, no atom it deletes, forbids or adds at that role and no atom it requires, deletes or
forbids at another role holds it, and atoms it adds at other roles do: the role keeps what the variable
names and the others record it, as the attestation 𝑦 of an AI agent, which a person who comes to
know it records [55]; a variable of an atom the schema deletes at one role and adds at another, as
the coins of a swap, is not local, and the request names it, as it names a variable that only the base’s
atoms hold and other roles record, the person who proposes choosing it, as Alice chooses whom
she introduces. A local variable is read at its role also where a conjunct of the constraint holds it
with a variable local to another role, as a count in a treasurer’s state compared with a count in their
community’s. A conjunct of the constraint is local to the role whose local variables it names, and
to every role where it names none; a conjunct that names variables local to two roles or more is of
the part’s condition (Definition 3.8). A schema-binding does not bind a local variable: the machine
of the person in that role finds a value for it in its own state, or computes it; the values it finds for
the variables read at its role, in each way its state allows, are the readings of its state, which its vote
yes carries; the initiator chooses readings under which the conjuncts of the condition hold, and the
36                                                                                Idit Keidar and Ehud Shapiro


commit tells them to every participant, whose effect is under them. Every other variable is bound
by the schema-binding, and a request names it. A quantifier of a schema is evaluated at the person
holding its anchor: an instance of it in a state is an assignment of the variables it binds under which
that person holds the anchor and the condition 𝜑 holds [16]; the instances of a conjunct with a
listed role give the people of the role, one for each instance, and the instances of a conjunct with
conditional literals give the atoms each adds or deletes. A safe formula of 𝐵, a constraint, and a
quantified conjunct, is local to a role where every atom of it, the anchors of its counts and sums
included, but the literals of a listed role in its conclusion, is held by that role, and every variable of it
is a role, a variable of the schema, a variable of its quantifiers or local to that role: the machine of the
person in that role evaluates it in its own state, as a contract for feeds and followers, in preparation,
forbids at Alice a conjunction of an atom and a constraint. A safe formula of 𝐵 that is local to no
role, its atoms held by two roles or more, each a role of the schema or a listed role its quantifier
binds, is of the part’s condition, as a formula that holds of every member of a listed role and reads
the base’s state and the member’s: the reading of a participant’s state then holds, beside the values
of the variables read at its role, the atoms of that state that the atoms of the formula held by its role
match, a variable local to another role and a variable the formula’s quantifiers bind matching any
value, and the initiator evaluates the formula on the readings, each atom, anchor, count and sum
taken in the reading of the person who holds it. A conjunct with a listed role, or with conditional
literals at a role other than the one holding its anchor, is local to its base; the base’s machine finds
its instances, the attempt names them, each restricted to the variables the conjunct’s literals name
and without repetition, and every participant takes them as arguments of the transaction. The
conjuncts of a schema that are local to their base and not to one role have one base, the base of the
schema; a schema with none has as its base the guarding role its pragma names before its button
(below) or, having no guarding role, its first role. A contract may name environment predicates [16],
whose atoms are over values the platform keeps current, which no schema adds or deletes and
a schema may require or forbid at a role and constrain a value variable by. One is a value the
contract declares and does not fix, as the fraction 𝑓 of a contract for backed community currencies
and the redundancy 𝑑 of a contract for feeds and followers, both in preparation: its value is given
when the platform is deployed, and the compiled agent holds the atom in its machine state from
startup, which the platform mapping reads and the view shows. The other is the day, today(𝑑): we
assume that every phone has the correct date, the date in UTC, and the compiled agent reads it
from its phone whenever it computes a condition, 𝑑 being that date, as the maturity of a bond is
read against the day [56]; a day that an act records is among its arguments, named by its proposer,
and each participant that requires it checks it against the date of its own phone. The specification
has no day, so today(𝑑) is no part of the machine state the platform mapping reads, and a part that
requires it is enabled in the specification whether or not its day has come; while the day does not
allow it the machine refuses it as unable, as it refuses any transaction it cannot carry out, so the
results of this section are of a contract’s acts on the days that allow them. The classes are those of
the contract’s equivalence, generated by relating two transactions of the same participants when
one schema-binding determines both [16], and each schema-binding determines a part [𝑡] 𝑗 of a
class for each list 𝐿 of instances, one list for each conjunct local to the base of the schema and not
                                                                                        [𝑡 ]
to one role: the set of transactions it determines with those instances, with 𝑓𝑝 𝑗 defined at 𝑑𝑝 iff
the schema-binding has the atoms it requires at 𝑝 in 𝑑𝑝 , with the atoms of the reflexive predicates
naming 𝑝 and the variables local to 𝑝’s role bound by them, in one way where the variable occurs
in an atom the schema deletes, those it forbids there not, the safe formulas and the conjuncts local
to 𝑝’s role holding at 𝑑𝑝 under that binding, the instances in 𝑑𝑝 of each conjunct of which 𝑝’s role
is the base, and that is not local to one role, being exactly those in 𝐿, and, for 𝑝 a person of a listed
Volition Elicitation                                                                                  37


role, the literals of the conjunct’s conclusion at 𝑝 under the instance in 𝐿 that binds 𝑝 holding at 𝑑𝑝 ,
and yielding 𝑑𝑝 less the deleted atoms, those of the conditional literals at each instance of their
conjunct, in 𝐿 or, for a conjunct local to 𝑝’s role, in 𝑑𝑝 , included, which 𝑑𝑝 need not hold [16], less
every atom of 𝑑𝑝 that a cleared atom matches, plus the added ones, likewise, as multisets, under
that binding, a multiplicity 𝑘 deleting or adding 𝑘 copies, and with the people in its guarding roles,
those in a listed guarding role being those of 𝐿, as its guard set; the readings of 𝑑𝑝 are the bindings
of the variables read at 𝑝’s role under which this holds, the part’s condition is the set of readings,
one of each participant, under which the conjuncts of the constraint that name variables local to
two roles or more, and the safe formulas local to no role, hold, and 𝑝’s effect is under the readings
chosen; its request name is the schema-binding, and, where the base of the schema is a guarding
role, its proposer is the person in the base. Such a contract is a local specification: unfriending,
whose schema determines the same transactions at the binding of either party first, is one class
with two parts, one guarded by each party. The opener of an unguarded part is the least, in the
order of identifiers, of the people in the first role of the schema-bindings that determine it, and
the opener of a guarded part determined by a schema whose base is not a guarding role is the
person in the base, who is not a guard of the part, as the ward whose state lists her guardians
opens the acts they take jointly [16]; the contract is a local specification where every other guarded
part of the class that a guard of such a part guards is determined likewise, with the same opener.
Locality is of the transactions a run can take (Section 3.2), so the compiler checks it on the states
that meet the invariants the contract’s paper proves, a contract with guardianships under GSC’s
Lemma Wards and guardians, by which every guardian of a ward is a person [16]. A schema may
have reading roles, a listed role among them, admitted where a guarding role requires an atom
naming the role, or the role is listed and its base guards, or the schema has no guarding role [16]:
the person in one is a participant of the transaction whose state does not change, as a stationary
agent is (Definition 2.1), its machine votes on the atoms the schema requires and forbids at it, and
it is asked nothing. The contract is effective (Definition 5.16) where, moreover, every variable of a
schema that occurs in an atom it requires or forbids, but a variable local to a role, also occurs in an
atom it deletes or adds, a variable inside a signed term or a name term included, so that a class has
finitely many parts, and the atoms each unguarded schema requires at its first role name every
other role and every variable of its deleted and added atoms, or the constraint gives the variable
finitely many values once those are bound, so that a state, a finite multiset of atoms, admits finitely
many unguarded parts its holder opens; and the base of a schema is a guarding role, or the person
in it opens the parts the schema determines, on a guard’s request where they are guarded, the base
of a schema with no guarding role being its first role, so that the person who proposes a part the
schema determines, its proposer or its opener, the base, finds the instances in their own state. The
introduction rule of a contract (Definition 3.18) gives each person 𝑝, for a part determined by a
schema-binding in which 𝑝’s role requires an atom naming an agent 𝑐 bound to a variable of the
schema or of an instance of the part and the person 𝑞 in another role gains an atom naming 𝑐, the
pair {𝑞, 𝑐}: at the commit 𝑝, who knows both, introduces them to each other, so that the machine
of a person who comes to know an agent can reach it; under GKK:3 (Section 7) Alice introduces
Carol and Bob. A contract may moreover name a predicate of arity one its introduction predicate 𝑒:
its introduction rule gives each person 𝑝 every two persons 𝑞 ≠ 𝑟 , both other than 𝑝, with 𝑒 (𝑞) and
𝑒 (𝑟 ) in 𝑝’s state, so the persons 𝑝 names by 𝑒 come to know each other.

The contract’s words. Beside its clauses and its schemas a contract carries the words its app
shows, its pragmas [16], and the compiler takes them into the platform it prints, so the app is
compiled from the contract (Section 8). The platform’s own words are four: the answers yes and no
of an approval question, and the reasons declined and unable of a notice. Every other word the
38                                                                            Idit Keidar and Ehud Shapiro


app shows is the contract’s, so the compiler prints a platform only from a contract with pragmas,
and refuses one with none; the checks that a contract is local, effective and grassroots are of its
schemas and need none.
   The interface pragmas may open with the contract’s look, ‘Look: 𝑖, 𝑓 ’, an icon 𝑖 and a family 𝑓
of colours [16], which the compiler carries into the platform it prints, among the pragmas of the
contract the platform exports (below), where the app and the site that show it read it [13]. The first
pragma after it names a tab of the app and what it lists, a predicate of the contract, named in the
line by its name, an underscore read as a space. The tab lists the atoms of that predicate in the state,
each with its count where the contract adds or deletes the predicate with a multiplicity, the view
carrying the whole state.
   Each other pragma names a clause and gives, on the clause’s line, the person who proposes, by
the clause’s name for them, and then, for each act they propose, its button, in brackets, bearing the
act’s name, and its fields, in angle brackets. A button is of the acts it names, and is named on one
line. The proposer’s machine finds in its own state every way to satisfy an act at the proposer’s
role: every binding of the variables of the atoms the schema requires there that a schema-binding
makes, distinct roles bound to distinct persons, under which the state holds those atoms and lacks
the atoms the schema forbids there that the binding determines. Each way is one button of the act,
ways that differ only in a variable local to the role being one, so a person’s agent_of (𝑐, 𝑦) binds
the attestation 𝑦 of their AI agent 𝑐, and a member’s member (⟨𝑎⟩ · 𝑛) binds a group’s leader 𝑎 and
its name 𝑛; a way binds also the instances of the conjuncts of which the proposer’s role is the
base, found in the same state, so the request names them. A field is for a variable of the schema
that those atoms do not bind and that is local to no other role — a text, a number, a new name, a
person the proposer’s state does not name — and the fields of a line name these variables, of the
acts its button names together, in the schemas’ order; the compiler refuses a line whose fields are
not these. The person named before a button is in a guarding role of its acts: in the base, where the
base guards, and then their proposer, or, where the base is an opener, a guard, who requests it, the
other guards being asked; a text or a new name their field takes is a speech act they sign then, new,
so that the binding is admissible and a name term formed from it is an argument of no atom of any
state [16]. A field is typed, a person by key or picked among the persons the viewer knows, and a
text by the type its variable carries in the schema where it carries one, as an email address or a
person’s name [16], which constrains nothing and which the field’s widget is told (Section 6.2). A
line follows for each other person who proposes, likewise, and for each person asked, their name, a
colon, and the question put to them; a question line belongs to the button line nearest above it, so
one person’s acts share a line only where no question follows them. A button is offered only to the
person named before it. A guarded act has a button, since a person proposes it; an unguarded one
is proposed by its opener’s machine and has none. An implementation pragma may make the acts
of a clause standing [16], as ‘Encounter: Alice stands on [Open place] until [Close place]’ does in a
contract for physical encounters in preparation: the person’s request of the act of the first button is
also their standing request (Definition 3.8) of every class of the clause’s acts that they propose, and
their request of the act of the second withdraws it; a standing act has no button, every guarding
role of it proposes it, and its persons are asked nothing, their machines voting yes on their standing
approvals.
