A    GRASSROOTS PROTOCOLS
Section 3 states that the protocol of every CVA platform is volitionally grassroots; the definitions
are these. We are particularly interested in grassroots protocols [28, 50, 57], which formalise the idea
that grassroots platforms can have multiple instances that form and operate independently, yet
may coalesce into ever-larger instances, possibly (but not necessarily) into a single global platform.
We recall the definitions of [28]. A prefix of a computation is a finite initial segment of it.
    Definition A.1 (Interleaving [28]). Let 𝑃, 𝑃 ′ ⊂ Π be disjoint and nonempty, 𝑟 = 𝑐 0, 𝑐 1, . . . a run
of F (𝑃) and 𝑟 ′ = 𝑑 0, 𝑑 1, . . . a run of F (𝑃 ′ ). An interleaving of 𝑟 and 𝑟 ′ is a sequence 𝑒 0, 𝑒 1, . . . of
configurations of F (𝑃 ∪ 𝑃 ′ ) for which there are non-decreasing index sequences (𝑖𝑘 ) and ( 𝑗𝑘 ) with
𝑖 0 = 𝑗0 = 0 such that for every 𝑘: (𝑒𝑘 )𝑝 = (𝑐𝑖𝑘 )𝑝 for every 𝑝 ∈ 𝑃; (𝑒𝑘 )𝑞 = (𝑑 𝑗𝑘 )𝑞 for every 𝑞 ∈ 𝑃 ′ ; and
if 𝑒𝑘+1 exists then exactly one of 𝑖𝑘+1 = 𝑖𝑘 + 1 with 𝑗𝑘+1 = 𝑗𝑘 , a 𝑃-step, or 𝑖𝑘+1 = 𝑖𝑘 with 𝑗𝑘+1 = 𝑗𝑘 + 1,
a 𝑃 ′ -step. If 𝑟 is finite of length 𝑛 then 𝑖𝑘 = 𝑛 for some 𝑘, and if 𝑟 is infinite then every index of 𝑟 is
reached by some 𝑖𝑘 ; likewise for 𝑟 ′ and ( 𝑗𝑘 ).
   Definition A.2 (Interaction, Interactive Run, First Interaction [28]). Let 𝑄 ⊂ Π; for a configuration
𝑐 over 𝑄 and 𝑅 ⊆ 𝑄, write 𝑐 |𝑅 for the restriction of 𝑐 to 𝑅. A transition 𝑒 → 𝑒 ′ of F (𝑄) is an
interaction between 𝑥 ≠ 𝑦 ∈ 𝑄 if 𝑒 | {𝑥 } ≠ 𝑒 ′ | {𝑥 } and 𝑒 | {𝑦 } ≠ 𝑒 ′ | {𝑦 } , and 𝑒 | {𝑥 } → 𝑒 ′ | {𝑥 } is not a
transition of F ({𝑥 }) or 𝑒 | {𝑦 } → 𝑒 ′ | {𝑦 } is not a transition of F ({𝑦}). It is an interaction between
disjoint nonempty 𝑃, 𝑃 ′ ⊆ 𝑄 if it is an interaction between some 𝑥 ∈ 𝑃 and some 𝑦 ∈ 𝑃 ′ . A run of
F (𝑄) is interactive between 𝑃 and 𝑃 ′ if some transition of it is an interaction between them, and
the first interaction of 𝑃 and 𝑃 ′ in it is the first such transition.
   Definition A.3 (Oblivious, Interactive, Grassroots [28]). A protocol F is oblivious if for every
𝑃, 𝑃 ′ ⊂ Π disjoint and nonempty every interleaving of a correct run of F (𝑃) and a correct run
of F (𝑃 ′ ) is a correct run of F (𝑃 ∪ 𝑃 ′ ); interactive if for every 𝑄 ⊂ Π and 𝑃, 𝑃 ′ ⊆ 𝑄 disjoint and
nonempty, every prefix of a correct run of F (𝑄) containing no interaction between 𝑃 and 𝑃 ′ is a
prefix of a correct run of F (𝑄) that contains one; and grassroots if it is oblivious and interactive.
   A protocol specified by transactions over a local-states function 𝑆 has, for each 𝑃 ⊂ Π, the set
𝑅(𝑃) of its transactions over some 𝑄 ⊆ 𝑃 ′ ⊆ 𝑃 and 𝑆 (𝑃 ′ ); the set is contained if for every 𝑃 ⊂ Π
every transaction in it with participants 𝑄 ⊆ 𝑃 and source in 𝑆 (𝑃)𝑄 is in 𝑅(𝑃) [28]. An agent may
leave — a person by death or incapacity, an AI agent by retirement — and interactivity is then asked
only of groups each holding an agent that has not left.
   Definition A.4 (Left, Interactive Among the Agents That Have Not Left [28]). Let F be a protocol
specified by volition-guarded transactions 𝑅, 𝑄 ⊂ Π, 𝑐 a configuration of F (𝑄) and 𝑥 ∈ 𝑄. Then 𝑥
has left in 𝑐 if no (𝑡, 𝑄 ′ ) ∈ 𝑅(𝑄) with 𝑥 ∈ 𝑄 ′ is machine-enabled in any configuration 𝑐 ′ of F (𝑄)
          ∗
with 𝑐 → − 𝑐 ′ . F is interactive among the agents that have not left if for every 𝑄 ⊂ Π and disjoint
nonempty 𝑃, 𝑃 ′ ⊆ 𝑄, every prefix of a correct run of F (𝑄) containing no interaction between 𝑃
and 𝑃 ′ , in whose last configuration some agent of 𝑃 and some agent of 𝑃 ′ have not left, is a prefix
of a correct run of F (𝑄) that contains one.
   Definition A.5 (Volitionally Grassroots [28]). A protocol F specified by volition-guarded transac-
tions is volitionally grassroots if it is oblivious, interactive among the agents that have not left, and,
for every 𝑃, 𝑃 ′ ⊂ Π disjoint and nonempty and every safe run of F (𝑃 ∪ 𝑃 ′ ) interactive between 𝑃
and 𝑃 ′ , the first interaction of 𝑃 and 𝑃 ′ is induced by a volition-guarded transaction (𝑡, 𝑄 ′ ) with
𝑄 ′ ∩ 𝑃 ≠ ∅ and 𝑄 ′ ∩ 𝑃 ′ ≠ ∅.
   Two groups thus become connected only by an act a person of each wills; an interactive protocol
is interactive among the agents that have not left, so a grassroots protocol meeting the condition
Volition Elicitation                                                                                 61


on first interactions is volitionally grassroots. An else transaction (Definition 2.6) is over a single
participant and so is never an interaction; a binary transaction with an else-branch would let an
agent of another group, who never wills it, trigger it, and could be a first interaction willed by
neither, which is why Definition 2.5 allows only unary ones.
   Two results of [28] are used below and stated without proof. The first is that an agent’s volitional
state, in any configuration of F (𝑃), holds only classes of transactions whose participants lie in 𝑃.
The second reduces obliviousness to a condition on those classes.

   Proposition A.6 ([28]). A protocol specified by a contained set of volition-guarded transactions
is oblivious provided that for every 𝑃, 𝑃 ′ ⊂ Π disjoint and nonempty, no equivalence class whose
transactions have participants in both 𝑃 and 𝑃 ′ is ever enabled in any interleaving of correct runs of
F (𝑃) and F (𝑃 ′ ).

A.1     An Example: Child-Safe Befriending
The example the Introduction summarises, as a speech-act schema [16].
Example: child-safe befriending. The child-safe social network, CSSN [54], extends GSN (Sec-
tion 7.2) and GnW, Guardians ’n’ Wards [55], its wards children and their guardians parents. GnW:4
reads “Carol, a ward of Alice, comes to know Dave, a ward of Bob, provided Alice knows Dave and
Bob, and enters a relation with Dave provided Carol and Dave know each other, Alice knows Dave
and is a friend of Bob; both by an act of Carol, Alice, Dave and Bob, Alice and Bob possibly one
person” [55]. Beside the predicates of GSN, child, of arity zero in a state, in the state of Carol says
that Carol is a child, and parenting(Carol) in the state of Alice says that Alice is a parent of Carol.
Every role requires identity and forbids retired (GnW:5), which the box does not show. The schema
by which two children become friends is:

      CSSN: Speech-Act Schemas, GnW:4 [54]

    4. friend (Carol, Dave), friend (Dave, Carol) ←
            child (Carol), knows(Carol, Dave), ¬friend (Carol, Dave), child (Dave),
            knows(Dave, Carol), ¬friend (Dave, Carol), parenting(Alice, Carol),
            knows(Alice, Dave), friend (Alice, Bob), ¬child (Alice), parenting(Bob, Dave),
            knows(Bob, Carol), friend (Bob, Alice), ¬child (Bob) guarded by Carol, Dave, Alice, Bob.

The act takes the will of the two children and of one parent of each, Alice of Carol and Bob of Dave,
who are friends, and changes the states of the two children. Alice and Bob may be one person, a
parent of both children, friend holding of every person of that person in CSSN. Where two friends
each parent both children, the befriending of the two has a part for each way of binding the parents,
each guarded by the four it binds (Section 3.2). A parent comes to parent a child by CSSN:1 and
GnW:2.

B     PROOFS
This appendix proves the results of Sections 3, 3.2, 5, 5.4 and 6.

   Proof of Proposition 3.6. Let F be the protocol of a CVA platform (Definition 3.4). We show
first that in every configuration of a safe run of F (𝑄) and for every 𝑝 ∈ 𝑄, every message in 𝑜 𝑝
has sender 𝑝 and a recipient in known𝑝 . It holds initially, 𝑜 𝑝 being empty. An app transaction adds
to 𝑜 𝑝 only messages message(𝑝, 𝑟, 𝑥) with 𝑟 ∈ known𝑝 and leaves known𝑝 as it is; Communicate
removes a message from 𝑜 𝑝 ; Discover and Introduce add to known𝑝 ; a volition change leaves both
62                                                                                 Idit Keidar and Ehud Shapiro


as they are. And an introduction intro(𝑞) that an agent 𝑟 sends has 𝑞 ∈ known𝑟 (Definition 3.4),
and, knowing never being undone, 𝑟 knows both 𝑞 and the recipient from then on.
     Second, which transitions are interactions. Let 𝑃, 𝑃 ′ ⊆ 𝑄 be disjoint and nonempty. An interaction
between 𝑃 and 𝑃 ′ is an interaction between some 𝑝 ∈ 𝑃 and 𝑞 ∈ 𝑃 ′ , and changes the states of
both (Definition A.2); a unary transaction changes the machine state of its one participant, and a
transaction taken removes its class from volitional states, which hold only classes of transactions
their agent participates in (Definition 2.3); so an interaction is a transition of a binary transaction
with a participant in each group, a Discover, an Introduce or a Communicate between some 𝑝 ∈ 𝑃
and 𝑞 ∈ 𝑃 ′ . A Discover(𝑝, 𝑞) or an Introduce(𝑝, 𝑞) is an interaction: it changes the states of both, and
it takes known𝑝 to a set containing 𝑞 ≠ 𝑝, which no machine state in 𝑆 ({𝑝}) holds, so 𝑒 | {𝑝 } → 𝑒 ′ | {𝑝 }
is not a transition of F ({𝑝}). Hence in a prefix of a safe run of F (𝑄) with no interaction between
𝑃 and 𝑃 ′ , known𝑝 ∩ 𝑃 ′ = ∅ for every 𝑝 ∈ 𝑃, since known𝑝 grows by Discovers and Introduces of
𝑝 only and one of 𝑝 with an agent of 𝑃 ′ is an interaction, and likewise known𝑞 ∩ 𝑃 = ∅ for every
𝑞 ∈ 𝑃 ′.
     Volitionally grassroots. Let 𝑃, 𝑃 ′ ⊂ Π be disjoint and nonempty, 𝑟 a safe run of F (𝑃 ∪𝑃 ′ ) interactive
between 𝑃 and 𝑃 ′ , and its first interaction between 𝑝 ∈ 𝑃 and 𝑞 ∈ 𝑃 ′ . A Communicate(𝑝, 𝑞, 𝑚)
requires 𝑚 = message(𝑝, 𝑞, 𝑥) ∈ 𝑜 𝑝 , so 𝑞 ∈ known𝑝 by the first part, where known𝑝 ∩ 𝑃 ′ = ∅ by
the second: it is not the first interaction, and nor is a Communicate(𝑞, 𝑝, 𝑚). An Introduce(𝑝, 𝑞)
requires an introduction intro(𝑞) in 𝑖 𝑝 , sent by an agent 𝑟 ∈ 𝑃 ∪ 𝑃 ′ that by the first part knows 𝑞
and 𝑝: if 𝑟 ∈ 𝑃, 𝑞 ∈ known𝑟 ∩ 𝑃 ′ , and if 𝑟 ∈ 𝑃 ′ , 𝑝 ∈ known𝑟 ∩ 𝑃, against the second part; so it is
not the first interaction either. The first interaction is therefore a Discover(𝑝, 𝑞), induced by the
volition-guarded transaction guarded by {𝑝, 𝑞}, which meets both 𝑃 and 𝑃 ′ .
     Oblivious. The transactions of the platform are contained (Appendix A): a transaction with
participants 𝑄 ⊆ 𝑃 and source in 𝑆 (𝑃)𝑄 has its targets in 𝑆 (𝑃)𝑄 , since Discover and Introduce add
to known only participants, an app transaction adds to the outbox only messages to known peers
and leaves known as it is, and the app states are 𝐴 whatever 𝑃 is; so it is a transaction over 𝑄 and
𝑆 (𝑃). By Proposition A.6 it then suffices that no class of transactions with participants in both 𝑃
and 𝑃 ′ is enabled in an interleaving of correct runs of F (𝑃) and F (𝑃 ′ ). Such a class is of a Discover,
an Introduce or a Communicate between some 𝑝 ∈ 𝑃 and 𝑞 ∈ 𝑃 ′ , and in an interleaving the state of
𝑝 is a state of F (𝑃). Discover(𝑝, 𝑞) is enabled only if 𝑝 wills it, and the volitional state of 𝑝 in F (𝑃)
holds only classes of transactions whose participants lie in 𝑃 (Definition 2.3), which 𝑞 does not.
Communicate(𝑝, 𝑞, 𝑚) is enabled only if message(𝑝, 𝑞, 𝑥) ∈ 𝑜 𝑝 , and 𝑜 𝑝 ⊆ 𝑀 (𝑃) in F (𝑃), whose
messages have their recipients in 𝑃. Introduce(𝑝, 𝑞) is enabled only if an introduction intro(𝑞) is in
𝑖 𝑝 , sent in F (𝑃) by an agent that knew 𝑞, and the known peers of a state of F (𝑃) lie in 𝑃, which 𝑞
does not. None is enabled, and the same holds with 𝑃 and 𝑃 ′ exchanged.
     Interactive. Let 𝑄 ⊂ Π, 𝑃, 𝑃 ′ ⊆ 𝑄 disjoint and nonempty, and 𝑟ˆ a prefix of a correct run of F (𝑄)
with no interaction between 𝑃 and 𝑃 ′ , ending in 𝑒; take 𝑝 ∈ 𝑃 and 𝑞 ∈ 𝑃 ′ . By the second part
𝑞 ∉ known𝑝 and 𝑝 ∉ known𝑞 in 𝑒, so Discover(𝑝, 𝑞) changes both their states, and it is enabled once
both will it. Extend 𝑟ˆ by a volition change of 𝑝 adding [Discover(𝑝, 𝑞)], one of 𝑞 adding it, and the
Discover, an interaction between 𝑃 and 𝑃 ′ . The result is a finite safe run of F (𝑄) with 𝑟ˆ as a prefix,
and by the Extension Lemma of [28] a prefix of a correct run of F (𝑄).
     F is thus oblivious and interactive, hence grassroots; interactive, it is interactive among the
agents that have not left (Definition A.4), and with the first interaction willed on both sides it is
volitionally grassroots.                                                                                     □


  Six facts about the runs of the canonical CVA platform (Definition 3.17) are used in the proofs of
Proposition 3.20 and Theorem 3.24.
Volition Elicitation                                                                                   63


    (i) Votes and outcomes. No transaction removes a term from a record, and a participant votes at
most once on an attempt: an initiator proposes an attempt once, its number growing, and votes on
it as it proposes it, and tells start(𝛼) once to each other participant, which reads it once, by Start;
Start votes, records a question, or, the attempt closed, neither; Answer records one answer to
a pending question; and Vote votes on it once it is answered, which ends it. So an attempt once
decided stays decided, and one on which a participant votes no is never decided, nor is one its
initiator refuses on its condition, the Tally that records its last yes either choosing its readings or
refusing it. An attempt is closed at its initiator only by its Propose, where the initiator votes no on
it, and otherwise by the first Tally, Commit, Yield or Wound that closes it, each requiring it not
closed and telling each other participant one outcome; at any other participant it is closed only by
that outcome’s Outcome.
    (ii) Locks. 𝐿𝑝 = 𝛼 exactly while 𝑝 has recorded voted(𝛼, yes) and not closed(𝛼): a vote yes, and
Propose voting yes, set 𝐿 := 𝛼 and require 𝐿 = ⊥ — Propose by its agent being free, Start by its
start not waiting, Vote explicitly — and 𝐿 returns to ⊥ only as 𝑝 closes 𝛼, by Tally, Commit, Yield
or Wound at its initiator and by Outcome elsewhere. While 𝐿𝑝 = 𝛼, 𝑠𝑝 does not change: 𝑠 changes
only by the Commit of an attempt at its initiator and by the Outcome that commits an attempt at
another participant, an attempt that 𝑝, being decided, voted yes on, so that 𝐿𝑝 is that attempt from
that vote to that Commit or Outcome. A vote yes being cast at a state in the domain of its effect
                                                                             [𝑡 ]
and carrying its readings, while 𝐿𝑝 is an attempt 𝛼 of [𝑡] 𝑗 , 𝑠𝑝 ∈ dom(𝑓𝑝 𝑗 ) and the readings 𝑝’s
                                                                                                [𝑡 ]
vote carried are those of 𝑠𝑝 ; so the readings 𝑤 chosen for 𝛼, once it is decided, have 𝑤 𝑝 ∈ 𝑔𝑝 𝑗 (𝑠𝑝 ),
the initiator’s own being of its state as it chooses, and the settled state applies the effect of every
decided lock.
   (iii) Questions. At most one question of a class is pending at an agent; while a question of [𝑡] is
pending at 𝑝, 𝐿𝑝 is no attempt of [𝑡], and, where it names no request, no approval of [𝑡] other than
𝑝’s answer to it is pending at 𝑝; and a guard is asked a question only by an attempt of which it
is not the requester. Start records a question of [𝑡] only where its start does not wait, so with 𝑝
free for [𝑡]: 𝐿𝑝 = ⊥ and none pending; where it names no request, only where no approval of [𝑡] is
pending at 𝑝; and only for an attempt that asks 𝑝. While it is pending, Propose and Start lock 𝑝
on no attempt of [𝑡] and record no second question of [𝑡], and Request makes no approval of [𝑡],
each requiring 𝑝 free for [𝑡]; Vote on another question locks 𝑝 on the attempt of that question, of
another class; and Vote on it ends it.
   (iv) Peers. Discover and Introduce are the only transactions that change known, and each adds
each of two agents to the other’s, so knowing is symmetric and never undone. Propose requires
the initiator to know every participant, so every participant an attempt reaches knows its initiator,
and the proviso that 𝑝 knows the agents it sends to holds of every Start, Vote, Tally, Yield and
Wound whose other conditions hold in a configuration of a run, Wound sending a yield to the
initiator of an attempt whose start its agent has read.
   (v) Priorities. For every attempt 𝛼, finitely many attempts have priority over 𝛼, and each attempt
is the 𝛼 of finitely many Wound transactions. The second first: an attempt 𝛾 is the 𝛼 of a Wound
at 𝑞 only while its start waits at 𝑞, or its question, answered yes, is pending there, once each; while
it does, 𝑞 records yielded(𝛽) or closes 𝛽 at the first Wound for each 𝛽 blocking it, and 𝑞 comes to
be blocked by an attempt 𝛾 has priority over only by Vote on a question pending before, finitely
many: Start of such an attempt waits behind 𝛾, so that no new question of it is raised, a question
of it answered yes waits behind 𝛾 where 𝛾 is a question, and 𝑞, not clear, proposes nothing. Now
the first. A rank is either the number of the Propose that sets it, fresh, or that of the initiator’s
last attempt of the same part, recorded wounded; an agent’s numbers grow, so finitely many fresh
ranks are at most the rank of 𝛼, and with 𝑃 finite finitely many pairs of an initiator and a rank give
64                                                                               Idit Keidar and Ehud Shapiro


priority over 𝛼. The attempts of such a pair are of one part, and each but the last is wounded, by a
Yield or a Wound for an attempt with priority over it, of a pair with priority over it. Were some
pair to have infinitely many attempts, the one of highest priority among them would be wounded
infinitely often by the finitely many attempts of the pairs with priority over it, each wounding
finitely often by the second part: so none has.
   (vi) Approvals. Every member 𝑔 of the guard set of an attempt 𝛼 of [𝑡] that votes yes on 𝛼 has an
approval of [𝑡] pending as it votes, and it stays pending until 𝛼 is decided or closed at its initiator.
The first: the initiator, a proposer, a guard, where the part of 𝛼 has no opener, proposes 𝛼 with
one pending; the requester of an attempt votes yes only while its request is pending; and an asked
guard votes yes by Start only where one is pending, its answer yes to an attempt naming the same
request where the attempt names one, or by Vote on its answer yes, which is one. The second: an
approval of [𝑡] pending at 𝑔 ends only as 𝑔 votes no on an attempt of a part of [𝑡] it guards, or
closes an attempt of [𝑡] committed, or refused and either naming that approval, or naming the
request that the attempt it answers names, or, naming none, one that 𝑔, a guard of its part, voted
yes on, or answered yes and did not vote on; a standing approval of [𝑡] ends for [𝑡] only as 𝑔 closes
an attempt of [𝑡] committed, or by Withdraw, which 𝐿𝑔 = 𝛼 excludes. From its vote yes on 𝛼 until
𝛼 closes at 𝑔, 𝐿𝑔 = 𝛼 (ii), so no other attempt of [𝑡] is decided, each needing 𝑔’s yes, and 𝑔 votes
on no other attempt of [𝑡], a start of one waiting while 𝑔 is not free for [𝑡] and no question of [𝑡]
being pending at 𝑔 (iii), any attempt it voted yes on before having closed at 𝑔 before 𝑔 voted on
𝛼. And an attempt naming the request that 𝛼 names is refused only at their initiator, the opener
of its part, which then deletes that request, so proposes no later attempt naming it, and proposes
nothing while 𝛼 is open.
   Proposition 3.20 (Atomic Commitment). In every safe run of P: (agreement) no attempt is
committed at one participant and aborted at another; and (validity) an attempt is committed at a
participant only if it is decided, each participant having voted yes on it holding no other attempt
prepared, at a machine state in the domain of its effect, a reading of which is among those chosen for it,
which meet the condition of its part, and each member of its guard set having voted yes on it with an
approval of its class pending, made by a Request, a Stand or an answer yes, which its person willed.
In every safe, live run of P: (termination) every attempt is eventually closed at each of its participants.
   Proof. Fix a safe run of P and an attempt 𝛼 = (𝑖, 𝑘, 𝜋, [𝑡] 𝑗 , 𝑟 ) proposed in it.
   Agreement. By (i), 𝛼 is closed at its initiator once, and at every other participant by the outcome
the initiator tells it on closing 𝛼: committed everywhere if the initiator commits it, and aborted
everywhere if it aborts it.
   Validity. 𝛼 is committed at its initiator by its Commit, which requires readings chosen for it,
in the condition of its part and one of each participant’s state as it voted (ii), a yes of every other
participant being then recorded, each recorded by its voter as it cast it; the initiator’s own was
recorded by its Propose. So 𝛼 is decided, and by agreement it is committed elsewhere only if
committed at its initiator. Every vote yes is cast with 𝐿 = ⊥ at a state in the domain of its voter’s
effect: Propose requires its agent free and votes yes only there, Start requires its start not waiting
and checks the state, and Vote checks both. By (vi) every member of the guard set of 𝛼 voted yes
on it with an approval of [𝑡] pending, made by a Request, a Stand or the then-branch of Answer,
each guarded by its agent, a member of its approval class, its request class or its standing class.
   Termination, the run being safe and live. Once its initiator closes 𝛼, it tells every other participant
the outcome, which reaches it by liveness and is read by Outcome, closing 𝛼 there; so it suffices
that every attempt is closed at its initiator. Call an attempt stuck if it is never closed at its initiator;
suppose some is, and let 𝛼 = (𝑖, 𝑘, 𝜋, [𝑡] 𝑗 , 𝑟 ) be a stuck attempt over which no stuck attempt has
priority, one existing as finitely many attempts have priority over any (v). Its initiator voted yes
Volition Elicitation                                                                                          65


on it, a no closing it at once. If 𝛼 has one participant, Commit is enabled from its Propose until
taken, and liveness takes it. Otherwise its initiator tells start(𝛼) to every other participant 𝑞, and
the Communicate of that message is enabled until taken, so by liveness it reaches 𝑞, which knows 𝑖
(iv). There the start waits only while 𝑞’s inbox holds the start of an attempt with priority over 𝛼, or
a question with priority over 𝛼 and answered yes is pending at 𝑞 — finitely many such attempts,
none stuck — or while 𝑞 is blocked by an attempt 𝛽. If 𝛼 has priority over 𝛽, Wound is enabled
until taken or 𝛽 is closed at 𝑞, and it closes 𝛽 or sends a yield to its initiator, whose Yield closes
𝛽 unless it is closed already; if not, 𝛽 is not stuck; either way 𝛽 is closed at its initiator and, its
outcome reaching 𝑞, at 𝑞. While the start waits, 𝑞 is blocked anew only by Vote on a question
pending before, finitely often, as in (v); so from some configuration on the start does not wait, and
Start on it is enabled until taken. So liveness takes it: 𝑞 votes on 𝛼 or records the question 𝛼. In the
last case 𝛼 is the one question of [𝑡] pending at 𝑞 (iii), and while it is pending and unanswered,
𝑞’s approval class on [𝑡] is enabled — by the then-branch of Answer where 𝑞 wills that class and
by its else-branch where not — with no other member enabled, Request requiring 𝑞 free for [𝑡];
so liveness takes Answer unless 𝛼 is closed at 𝑞 first, and then Vote, the answer yes waiting, as
the start did, only finitely long. Each vote reaches 𝑖 likewise and is read by Tally. No participant
votes no, and no Yield or Wound closes 𝛼, since each would close it; and no participant closes 𝛼, 𝑖
telling no outcome; so every participant votes yes, the Tally of the last of these votes records a
yes of every other participant and chooses readings or refuses 𝛼, closing it, and Commit, enabled
from the choice until taken, closes 𝛼, against the supposition. So no attempt is stuck.                □
   The mapping for Theorem 3.24 reads the specification’s machine states off the settled states and
its volitions off the volitional states and the approvals.
   Definition B.1 (CVA Implementation). The CVA implementation of a local volition-guarded speci-
fication over 𝑃 ⊂ Π is the function 𝜎 taking a configuration 𝑐 of its canonical CVA platform over
𝑃 to the agent configuration over 𝑃 with, at each 𝑝 ∈ 𝑃, machine state the settled state of 𝑝 in 𝑐,
and volitional state the set of classes [𝑡] of which 𝑝 is a guard such that 𝐿𝑝 is not an attempt of [𝑡]
decided in 𝑐 and either (A) the volitional state of 𝑝 holds its approval class on [𝑡], its request class
on a request holding [𝑡] or its standing class on a standing request holding [𝑡], or (B) an approval
of [𝑡] is pending at 𝑝.
  Theorem 3.24 (CVA Implementation). The implementation of volition-guarded specifications by
CVA is correct, and complete for strongly fair runs.
   Proof. Fix a local volition-guarded specification and 𝑃 ⊂ Π, with P and F (𝑃) as in Section 3.2,
and let 𝜎 be the mapping of Definition B.1. The initial configuration has no locks, no approvals and
no wills, so 𝜎 maps it to the initial agent configuration: every settled state is 𝑠 0 and every volitional
state is empty; (P, 𝜎) is an implementation of F (𝑃) (Definition 2.14).
   Safety, per transition 𝑐 → 𝑐 ′ .
   The deciding transition. Let the transition decide an attempt 𝛼 = (𝑖, 𝑘, 𝜋, [𝑡] 𝑗 , 𝑟 ): the Propose
of 𝛼 voting yes, if 𝑄 ( [𝑡]) = {𝑖}, and otherwise the Tally at 𝑖 that records the last of the other
participants’ yes votes and chooses readings; let 𝑤 be the readings chosen. In the first case, before

𝑞 of 𝛼 has voted yes on 𝛼 and has 𝐿𝑞 = 𝛼 by (ii), 𝛼 not yet being closed at 𝑞 — its initiator closes it,

enabled in 𝜎 (𝑐) (Definition 2.13).
   Other votes yes. A Propose of an attempt of more than one participant voting yes, or a Vote or
Start casting a yes on an attempt 𝛼 of [𝑡], sets 𝐿𝑝 := 𝛼, undecided, decides no attempt, and fulfils
no will, Propose being unguarded: no settled state changes, and an undecided lock leaves both
clauses as they were: a stutter.
   Approvals. A Request of 𝑝 of 𝜌 fulfils 𝑝’s request class on 𝜌, or its approval class on [𝑡] where
𝜌 = {[𝑡]}, a Stand of 𝜌 fulfils 𝑝’s standing class on 𝜌, and the then-branch of Answer on a question
of [𝑡] fulfils 𝑝’s approval class on [𝑡]; so 𝑝, which held each class the approval approves by (A)
before — 𝐿𝑝 being ⊥ for a Request or a Stand, which require 𝑝 free, and no attempt of [𝑡] for
an Answer (iii) — holds it by (B) after, a standing approval being fresh, with no commit since. A
Withdraw of 𝜌 ends 𝑝’s standing approval of 𝜌, 𝐿𝑝 being no attempt of a class of 𝜌: a volition
change of 𝑝, or a stutter. Nothing else 𝜎 reads changes: a stutter. The else-branch of Answer fulfils
no will and records the answer no, which 𝜎 does not read; and changes no class its agent holds and
no approval: a stutter.
   Votes no. A vote no, by Start, by Vote, or by a Propose that also closes its attempt, locks nothing,
changes no settled state, its attempt never being decided (i), and fulfils no will; where its agent is a
guard of the attempt’s part it ends the agent’s approvals of [𝑡], so that the agent may cease to hold
[𝑡]: a volition change of the agent, or a stutter, but for the approvals the closing ends, as below.
   Closing. A Propose voting no, a Tally, a Commit, a Yield, a Wound of its agent’s own attempt,
or an Outcome, that closes 𝛼 at 𝑝 sets 𝐿𝑝 := ⊥ where 𝐿𝑝 = 𝛼, and applies 𝑝’s effect where 𝛼 is
committed: if 𝛼 is decided the effect moves from the settled state into 𝑠𝑝 , and if not, 𝛼 is aborted
(Proposition 3.20) and the lock it releases carried no effect. No settled state changes. Where it ends
approvals of 𝑝 — on a commit, every approval of [𝑡], a standing approval ceasing to approve [𝑡],
and on a refusal the request the attempt names or, naming none, every approval of [𝑡] where 𝑝
voted or answered yes on the attempt — 𝑝 may cease to hold their classes, and where it releases a
decided lock, (A) may resume for [𝑡]: a volition change of 𝑝, or a stutter.
   The rest. A Start that records a question or reads the start of an attempt closed at its agent, a
Tally that records a yes without deciding or refusing its attempt or reads the vote of an attempt
closed at its agent, a Wound that sends a yield, and a Yield that reads the yield of an attempt closed
or decided at its agent, change nothing 𝜎 reads, numbers and ranks included: stutters. Discover,
Introduce, Communicate, Introduce peers, Ready and Readied change known, the messages and
the record of introductions and readiness, which 𝜎 does not read: stutters. A volition change of 𝑝
changes which approval and request classes 𝑝 holds, hence at most which classes 𝜎 gives 𝑝, by (A):
a volition change of 𝑝, or a stutter.
   Every safe run of P therefore maps to a safe run of F (𝑃), and a member of [𝑡] occurs in 𝜎 (𝜌)
only at a transition deciding an attempt of [𝑡].

   No refusals. A vote no on an attempt of [𝑡] 𝑗 cast in the suffix by Start or Propose on its agent’s

there (Proposition 3.20), and then 𝑔 has committed an attempt since, or 𝑞’s Ready on 𝛽 is enabled
until taken and reaches 𝑔 likewise; so likewise 𝑔 takes infinitely many Propose transactions for
attempts of [𝑡] 𝑗 . Where [𝑡] 𝑗 is guarded and has an opener, each of its guards knows 𝑔, which knows
it, and every guarded part of [𝑡] that a guard of [𝑡] 𝑗 guards has the opener 𝑔. A request of such a
guard, made with it knowing 𝑔, is delivered by liveness, and 𝑔 holds it from then until it deletes
it, at the refusal of an attempt naming it or at a commit of a class of it, either of which ends it at
its guard as that reads the outcome. Let ℎ be a guard of [𝑡] 𝑗 ; by the first part an approval of [𝑡] is
pending at ℎ in infinitely many configurations of the suffix. If ℎ makes infinitely many requests
holding [𝑡], each is delivered, so 𝑔 holds a request of [𝑡] in infinitely many configurations. If ℎ
gives infinitely many answers yes to attempts of [𝑡], these are attempts of 𝑔 naming requests of [𝑡],
infinitely many of them proposed in the suffix, each in a configuration in which 𝑔 holds the request
it names. Otherwise one approval of [𝑡] is pending at ℎ from some configuration on: a request of ℎ,
which 𝑔 holds for good from its delivery, or an answer yes of ℎ to an attempt naming a request,
which 𝑔 holds for good, the answer ending as ℎ reads the refusal of an attempt naming it. So 𝑔
holds a request of [𝑡] in infinitely many configurations; 𝜌 being unobstructed, 𝑔 is free for [𝑡] and
clear in infinitely many of them, in each of which a Propose of 𝑔 for an attempt of [𝑡] 𝑗 naming a
request it holds is enabled; so, 𝜌 being strongly fair, 𝑔 takes infinitely many Propose transactions
for attempts of [𝑡] 𝑗 , no attempt of [𝑡] being decided.
    Decision. Every attempt of [𝑡] 𝑗 proposed after 𝑐 1 is closed at its initiator (Proposition 3.20), not
by a refusal, of which there is none after 𝑐 1 , nor by Commit, which follows its decision, against
the hypothesis; so it is wounded, by a Yield or a Wound. Infinitely many being proposed, by the
finitely many proposers of [𝑡] 𝑗 or its opener, some agent 𝑔 initiates infinitely many of them; each
from its second on retries a wounded one, so all have one rank, and each is wounded in favour
of an attempt with priority over it, of which there are finitely many, each the 𝛼 of finitely many
Wound transactions (v): so finitely many are wounded, a contradiction. Hence 𝜎 (𝜌) is live, and
with safety, correct.
    Completeness. Let 𝑟 = 𝑐 0, 𝑐 1, . . . be a correct run of F (𝑃) that is strongly fair. We build a correct
run 𝑟 ′ of P with 𝜎 (𝑟 ′ ) = 𝑟 , in stages, keeping between stages the invariant that the current
configuration 𝑒 of P is quiescent for the current configuration 𝑐 of 𝑟 : every agent knows every other;
every outbox is empty, every inbox holds introductions only, and no Introduce peers is enabled;
every lock is ⊥, every attempt proposed is closed at each of its participants, and no approval is
pending; 𝑠𝑝 = 𝑐𝑚    𝑝 for every 𝑝; and the volitional state of 𝑝 in 𝑒 holds exactly 𝑝’s approval classes on
the members of 𝑐 𝑝𝑣 . A quiescent 𝑒 has 𝜎 (𝑒) = 𝑐: with no locks, the settled state of 𝑝 is 𝑠𝑝 , and clause
(A) yields 𝑐 𝑝𝑣 ; and every agent is clear in it, its inbox holding no start.
    Start. From the initial configuration, for each pair 𝑝 ≠ 𝑞 of 𝑃 in turn, a volition change of 𝑝 and
one of 𝑞 add the class of Discover(𝑝, 𝑞), and the Discover is taken; then each Introduce peers
enabled is taken, with the Communicates of its introductions; each step is a stutter under 𝜎, which
reads neither known, the Discover classes, the messages nor the record of introductions, and the
result is quiescent for 𝑐 0 .

     Idle. A guard 𝑔 of a guarded part [𝑢] 𝑗 of a class [𝑢] not enabled in 𝑐 𝑖+1 , a proposer of it where
[𝑢] 𝑗 has no opener, that holds its approval class on [𝑢] and is ready for [𝑢] 𝑗 has a Request of
{[𝑢]} enabled in a quiescent configuration, and the opener 𝑔 of an unguarded part [𝑢] 𝑗 of such a
class that is ready for [𝑢] 𝑗 has a Propose; neither may stay enabled forever. For each such pair
(𝑔, [𝑢] 𝑗 ), taken in a fair order over the countably many of them — a pair enabled at infinitely many
quiescent configurations being taken infinitely often — and interleaved with the stages above, run
an idle attempt. Where [𝑢] 𝑗 is guarded, take first 𝑔’s Request of {[𝑢]}, to itself, or to the opener 𝑜
of [𝑢] 𝑗 with the Communicate of the request, moving 𝑔 from (A) to (B). Let the initiator 𝑖 be 𝑔, or 𝑜
where [𝑢] 𝑗 has an opener, and take its Propose of an attempt 𝛼 of [𝑢] 𝑗 , naming 𝑔’s request where
𝑖 = 𝑜; let 𝐺 = 𝐺𝛼 . As [𝑢] is not enabled in 𝑐 𝑖+1 , some participant 𝑞 refuses 𝛼 with no will fulfilled: a
participant not ready for [𝑢] 𝑗 , its machine state outside the domain of its effect, votes no, by Start,
or by Propose where it is 𝑜; and if every participant is ready and some member 𝑞 of 𝐺 does not
will its approval class on [𝑢], 𝑔 willing its own, then 𝑞, a guard other than the requester of 𝛼, is
asked, and it records the question by Start, the answer no by the else-branch of Answer, and votes
no by Vote. Where 𝑞 ≠ 𝑖, take the Communicate of start(𝛼) to 𝑞, its transactions up to its vote, and
the Communicate of the vote. Otherwise every participant is ready and every member of 𝐺 wills
its approval class on [𝑢], so no readings of their states meet the condition of [𝑢] 𝑗 , a member of it
being otherwise enabled in 𝑐 𝑖+1 , by locality, and 𝑖 refuses 𝛼 on its condition: take, for each other

participant in turn, the Communicate of start(𝛼) to it, its transactions up to its vote yes, an asked
guard answering by the then-branch of Answer, the Communicate of the vote, and, but for the
last, 𝑖’s Tally of it. Then take a volition change of 𝑔, where [𝑢] 𝑗 is guarded, and of every guard that
answered yes, adding its approval class on [𝑢] back; then 𝑖’s Tally of the vote no, or of the last
yes, which aborts 𝛼, unless 𝑖 aborted it by its Propose; and, for every participant other than 𝑖, the
Communicate of the outcome and its Outcome, and, for every one whose start is still undelivered,
then the Communicate of start(𝛼) and its Start, which does nothing. Every step is a stutter: 𝑔
holds [𝑢] under 𝜎 throughout, by (B) from its Request and by (A) as well once its approval class is
back, which is before the refusal ends its approval, and likewise a guard that answered yes, from
its answer; a vote yes locks its voter on 𝛼, which is never decided; and no other agent’s holding
changes. The result is again quiescent for 𝑐 𝑖+1 . A pair whose class is enabled in 𝑐 𝑖+1 is not idled
there.
   Finite runs. If 𝑟 is finite, the stages after its last configuration consist of idle attempts.
   The run 𝑟 ′ so built is safe, and 𝜎 (𝑟 ′ ), with stutters removed, is 𝑟 . Each stage is finite and begins
and ends at a quiescent configuration, where every agent is free for every class and clear; within a
stage an agent’s settled state changes only at the deciding vote, and its approval classes only by
fulfilment, by the removals that follow that vote and by an idle attempt’s restoring one; so an agent
whose settled state is in the domain of its effect in a part and which holds its approval class on the
part’s class, has an approval of it pending, or opens the part, at a configuration of a stage does so
at the stage’s first or last configuration, or the stage is an idle attempt of that part. A Propose or
Request enabled at a configuration of a stage is likewise enabled at its first or last configuration,
except a Propose that needs an approval or a request, which is enabled only within the stage that
makes the approval, and is taken there. Every attempt is closed within its stage, every message
sent but an introduction is read within it, an introduction is delivered within it, and every question
raised is answered within it. So 𝑟 ′ is connected, every agent knowing every other; unobstructed, its
configurations of an agent at a state in the domain of its effect in a part that meet a condition of
Definition 3.21 lying in infinitely many stages if they are infinitely many, each ending free and
clear; steady, since at its quiescent configurations, where the settled states are the machine states of
𝑟 and an agent is for a class exactly when it holds its approval class, a part of a class holds with all
its guards for the class only where the class is enabled in 𝑟 , so that a class for which this holds from
some configuration on, with no attempt of it decided, would stay enabled in 𝑟 with no member
taken, against 𝑟 being live; resolute, no agent holding a request class on a request of two classes or
more, and no question being put to an agent at which a request is pending; and strongly fair and
live: a Discover class is enabled only at the start; an Introduce never is, every agent knowing every
other; an Introduce peers or a Ready class is taken in the stage that enables it; a Request, or the
Propose of the opener of an unguarded part, enabled at infinitely many configurations is enabled
at infinitely many quiescent ones, where it is taken by an idle attempt in its turn, at those where its
class is not enabled in 𝑟 , or its class is enabled in infinitely many configurations of 𝑟 , which, being
strongly fair, takes it in infinitely many, each a stage that decides an attempt of the class with its
agent as a participant, whose lock keeps it disabled there; and a Propose that needs an approval
or a request is taken in the stage that enables it. Hence 𝑟 ′ is a correct run of P under its fairness
condition with 𝜎 (𝑟 ′ ) = 𝑟 , and 𝜎 is complete for strongly fair runs.                                    □


   Theorem 5.14 (Volitional Soundness). Let 𝑐 1 → 𝑐 2 → · · · be a proper safe run of the vmaGLP
transition system over 𝑃 and 𝑀, 𝑝 ∈ 𝑃, and 𝑋 a question of 𝑝’s person in 𝑐 𝑖 . Then the writer 𝑋 occurs
in 𝑐 𝑖 in 𝐻𝑝 and nowhere else, and a transition 𝑐 𝑖 → 𝑐 𝑖+1 that adds an assignment 𝑋 ? := 𝑡 to a readers
substitution is the Answer of 𝑝 to 𝑋 .
Volition Elicitation                                                                                  71


   Proof. We show first that in every configuration 𝑐 𝑘 of the run (i) every writer a person holds
occurs in that person’s 𝐻 and nowhere else, and (ii) every writer in the term of an assignment in a
readers substitution occurs in that term once and nowhere else. In 𝑐 1 no person holds a variable.
Suppose both hold in 𝑐 𝑘 . An Ask introduces a writer occurring nowhere in 𝑐 𝑘 and gives its person
one end of it, the other end going to the asked goal. An Answer to a question removes it from
the variables its person holds, adds an assignment to its reader, which the person does not hold,
and introduces variables occurring nowhere in 𝑐 𝑘 : writers the person holds, their readers in the
answer, and writers in the answer for the program to write, their readers held by the person. A
Communicate to a reader a person holds gives that person the variables of the term 𝑇ˆ it delivers and
the assignment, shown; each writer of 𝑇ˆ occurred, by (ii), once in the term of one of the assignments
used in forming 𝑇ˆ and nowhere else, and those assignments, communicated with it, are removed
(Definition 4.10). A Reduce at an agent 𝑞 renames its clause apart, and the run being proper, the
variables of the renamed clause occur nowhere in 𝑐 𝑘 or earlier, at any agent; its writer mgu 𝜎ˆ binds
writers of the goal and of the renamed head, none of them held by a person, and a writer in a term it
assigns is a variable of the renamed clause or of the goal, occurring in that term once and nowhere
else, by SRSW and by the SO of the goal, which the reduction removes. Any other Communicate,
and a Cold-call, put into a goal a term already in 𝑐 𝑘 , which by (i) holds no writer a person holds.
Hence both hold in 𝑐 𝑘+1 , and (i) holds in 𝑐 𝑖 .
   Now let 𝑐 𝑖 → 𝑐 𝑖+1 add 𝑋 ? := 𝑡 to a readers substitution. An Ask, a Communicate and a Cold-call
add no assignment. A Reduce adds 𝜎?,   ˆ whose domain is the readers of the writers 𝜎ˆ binds, writers
of the goal and of the renamed head, and by (i) 𝑋 is neither. So the transition is an Answer to a
question 𝑍 , adding 𝑍 ? := 𝑡; hence 𝑍 = 𝑋 , and 𝑋 being among the questions of 𝑝’s person and of no
other, it is the Answer of 𝑝 to 𝑋 .                                                                 □
  Theorem 5.19 (Compilation of the Canonical Platform). For every effective local volition-
guarded specification and 𝑃 ⊂ Π, the platform mapping is a correct implementation of P, the transition
system of its canonical platform with a computable introduction rule or none over 𝑃 under its fairness
condition (Definition 3.22), by the vmaGLP transition system over 𝑃 and ⌈Θ⌉, under the fairness
condition that a run is proper, no approval question stays open in it, and its image under the platform
mapping is connected, unobstructed, steady and resolute.
   Proof. Fix an effective local volition-guarded specification, its canonical platform Θ and 𝑃 ⊂ Π;
write 𝑇 𝑆 ′ for the vmaGLP transition system over 𝑃 and ⌈Θ⌉, and 𝜎 for the platform mapping. 𝜎
maps the initial configuration of 𝑇 𝑆 ′ to that of P: each agent’s resolvent is the initial goal, so no
hello is sent, no message is on a stream, nothing is read and nothing is willed, and the agent goal,
once called, holds (𝑠 0, 0, ⊥, ∅).
   Four facts about the configurations of a safe run of 𝑇 𝑆 ′ . First, a request, a message and an answer
each pass from where they are written — the person’s answer, the network input stream, the answer
to an approval question — through the merge to the head of the agent goal’s stream, where the
clause of their kind reads them and computes in its body the condition of the transaction they
put up, on the agent state the clause was called with; the agent goal is one goal, so no dispatch
intervenes before the dispatch procedure’s reduction, and the app state and the starts held waiting
do not change between the reading of a trigger and its dispatch. Second, where a hello is hello or
ihello, the two of an exchange being of one kind, a peer an agent has sent a hello to and received
one from is one with which it has exchanged hellos in 𝜎, a hello received having been sent, and not
conversely until the peer’s hello arrives: a condition’s test that a peer is known, true at the agent,
is true in 𝜎. Third, an agent reads the start of an attempt only after it has sent its sender a hello
and read the sender’s: the sender sends only to a peer it has received a hello from and sent its own
to, which precedes the start on its output stream, and the messages of one stream are delivered
72                                                                            Idit Keidar and Ehud Shapiro


and read in order; so an agent knows the sender of every start it reads, and, knowing being never
undone, every agent Start, Vote and Tally send to. Fourth, an agent sends a cargo to a recipient at
most once, by fact (i) of the proofs for Section 3.2 and its records of yields and introductions, so no
two messages on the streams are one message of P.
     Safety, per transition of 𝑇 𝑆 ′ . An Ask, a Communicate to a reader the person holds or of an
assignment along a stream, a Reduce of a merge goal, the agent’s guarded clause, the clause that
asks an approval question, the goal it hands its answer to, or a goal computing a condition or
an effect, and a Cold-call, an app message it delivers staying in 𝑜 𝑝 until its recipient’s agent goal
reads it, change nothing 𝜎 reads: stutters. An Answer of 𝑝 to its request question is a stutter, the
request being unread, except with a request of an acquaintance part with 𝑞 ∈ 𝑃: then it adds
[Discover(𝑝, 𝑞)], a volition change, unless 𝑞 ∈ known𝑝 or the class is already there; the person’s
yes to the question an arriving hello asks is such a request, and their no a stutter. An Answer to
an approval question is a stutter, the answer being unread.
     A Reduce of the agent goal’s clause reading a request propose(𝑁 ), 𝑁 naming a guarded part
that 𝑝 guards, adds to 𝑝’s volitional state its approval class on [𝑡] where the request puts up the
Request of {[𝑡]} and its request class on 𝜌 𝑁 where it puts up the Request of 𝜌 𝑁 of two or more
classes, and one reading the answer yes to the approval question of an attempt of [𝑡] adds 𝑝’s
approval class on [𝑡] where the condition of the Answer, but for its test of known peers, holds at
𝑝’s machine state: a volition change of 𝑝, the agent goal reading one trigger at a time; otherwise it
is a stutter, and so is a dispatch that holds a request or puts one up again. Reading an app message
𝑚 that 𝑞 sent, it is Communicate(𝑞, 𝑝, 𝑚), 𝑚 leaving 𝑜𝑞 and entering 𝑖 𝑝 ; reading a hello, an answer
no or an own trigger, it is a stutter.
     A Reduce of a dispatch clause taking a transaction is that transaction of P at the configuration 𝜎
reads: its condition holds there, by the first fact on the app state and the inbox and by the second
and the third on the peers it requires known. Request and the then-branch of Answer are guarded
by 𝑝, and 𝑝’s approval class on [𝑡], or its request class on 𝜌 𝑁 , is in its volitional state, read with
its condition holding, so the transaction is enabled, and the reduction removes the class, fulfilled
(Definition 2.4). The else-branch of Answer, taken on the answer no, is enabled, 𝑝 holding no
approval class while the agent goal reads an answer no. Start, Tally, Yield and Outcome are
unguarded and take a message in 𝑖 𝑝 , which they remove; Propose, Vote, Commit and Wound,
taken as its own transactions, are unguarded, Wound leaving the start it reads in 𝑖 𝑝 , and a Propose
naming a request it holds, in 𝑖 𝑝 , deleting it where it votes no. The new app state and the messages
the effect yields, addressed to peers known at the agent and so in 𝜎, are the transaction’s effect. A
Reduce of a dispatch clause holding a start waiting, or a request, is a stutter, the message staying in
𝑖 𝑝 . A Reduce of a dropping clause on a request or an answer yes is a volition change of 𝑝 removing
the class its reading added, and a stutter where it added none; on an answer no it is a stutter, and
so is the agent goal’s finding none of its own transactions holding. A Reduce of the request clause
on a request of an acquaintance part with 𝑞 sending hello is a stutter where 𝑞 has not sent hello
to 𝑝 or 𝑞 ∈ known𝑝 already, and otherwise Discover(𝑝, 𝑞): both will it, each having a pending
request of the acquaintance with the other, 𝑞’s made by its person or by their yes to 𝑝’s hello, with
𝑞 ∉ known𝑝 , and after it known𝑝 and known𝑞 each gain the other and both volitional states lose
the class. A Reduce of the clause reading an introduction intro(𝑞) and sending ihello is a stutter
where 𝑞 has not sent ihello to 𝑝 or 𝑞 ∈ known𝑝 already, and otherwise Introduce(𝑝, 𝑞): each has
read an introduction of the other, both of which leave the inboxes, and after it known𝑝 and known𝑞
each gain the other; the reduction recording an arriving ihello is a stutter. A Reduce of a dispatch
clause taking Introduce peers is that transaction, its introductions addressed to known peers.
Every safe run of 𝑇 𝑆 ′ therefore maps to a safe run of P.
Volition Elicitation                                                                                  73


   Liveness. Let 𝑟 ′ be a proper correct run of 𝑇 𝑆 ′ in which no approval question stays open and
whose image 𝑟 = 𝜎 (𝑟 ′ ) is connected, unobstructed, steady and resolute. In 𝑟 ′ every element written
to a stream is read by the agent goal’s clause of its kind: the Communicate of its assignment and the
reductions of the merge goals are enabled until taken and the merge is fair, and once the element
heads the agent goal’s stream the clause of its kind reduces, by Monotonicity and the clause order
as for the requests of Section 5; its condition is then bound, being computable and terminating, and
the dispatch clause reduces. Own triggers are elements of that stream too, so the agent goal reads
every other trigger however many own triggers it writes. It reads an own trigger after each other
trigger it reads, the one it writes then or one it wrote before and has not read, and after each own
transaction it takes, and on each it takes one of its own transactions whose condition holds, if one
does, by a reduction of a dispatch clause, enabled until taken. Suppose a class of P is enabled in
every configuration of a suffix of 𝑟 and no member of it occurs there.
   Communicate(𝑝, 𝑞, 𝑚): 𝑚 is on 𝑝’s output stream, the Cold-calls of the messages before it and
then of 𝑚 are each enabled until taken, so 𝑚 is delivered and 𝑞’s agent goal reads it. The class of
Discover(𝑝, 𝑞) and Introduce(𝑝, 𝑞): enabled through Discover, both will it, so each has a pending
request of an acquaintance part with the other, and each request is read and its hello sent, if not
before, 𝑞 not being known at 𝑝 nor 𝑝 at 𝑞, the second sending being a Discover(𝑝, 𝑞); enabled
through Introduce, each has read an introduction of the other and so sent ihello, if not before, the
second sending being an Introduce(𝑝, 𝑞). Tally, Yield, Outcome or Readied on a message 𝑚 ∈ 𝑖 𝑝 :
𝑚 is read and not yet dispatched, and its dispatch takes it. Start on 𝑚: read and not yet dispatched,
the dispatch takes it, 𝑚 not waiting; held waiting, its waiting held at the state at which it was last
put up and does not hold now, so a dispatch has since changed the app state or consumed a start,
after which it was put up again and taken.
   A Propose, a Vote on a question pending at 𝑝, a Commit, a Wound, a Ready, or an Introduce
peers: at each configuration of the suffix an instance of the class has its condition holding at the
agent, where it tests a known peer once the peer’s hello has arrived, which it does, the peer being
known in 𝑟 . Only the agent goal’s reading of a trigger and its dispatches change that condition,
and after each it reads an own trigger, so from the suffix’s first configuration and the arrival of that
hello on it reads own triggers without end and takes an own transaction on each, an instance of
the class that has waited longest; a class taken waits from then on less long than this one, and the
classes that have waited longer than it are finitely many — those taken, or found holding, before
it began to wait, finitely many at each step, the unguarded parts an agent opens at a state, the
approvals pending at it, the requests it holds, the questions pending at it, the attempts it initiated
and the starts it holds being finitely many — so within finitely many of these steps the dispatch
takes an instance of it.
   𝑝’s approval class on [𝑡], whose members are 𝑝’s Request of {[𝑡]} and its Answer on the
questions of [𝑡], or its request class on a request 𝜌, whose members are its Request transactions of
𝜌. If Request is enabled in infinitely many configurations of the suffix, 𝑝 holds the class in them,
so in infinitely many a request propose(𝑁 ) that puts up the Request of {[𝑡]} or of 𝜌 is read or
held at 𝑝, 𝑝 being free for its classes and ready for a part it names; a held request is put up again
after every dispatch, so it is dispatched at such a configuration within finitely many steps, and that
dispatch takes the transaction unless a peer its condition tests is known in 𝑟 and not yet at the agent,
which, each hello arriving, happens finitely often. Otherwise, from some configuration on, Answer
is enabled throughout, on a question 𝛼 of [𝑡] pending at 𝑝 and unanswered, at most one being
pending, by fact (iii) of the proofs for Section 3.2; 𝛼 is never closed at 𝑝, the configuration after its
closing enabling neither Request of {[𝑡]}, by the supposition, nor Answer, no Start having yet
raised another question. So the approval question Start raised on 𝛼 is neither answered and read
74                                                                              Idit Keidar and Ehud Shapiro


nor closed: it is asked, and is open until answered; it is answered, no approval question staying
open; the answer is read; and the dispatch takes the then-branch on yes and the else-branch on no.
   In every case a member of the class occurs in the suffix: 𝑟 is live.
   Fairness. 𝑟 is connected, unobstructed, steady and resolute by hypothesis, and strongly fair. A
Request of 𝑝 to 𝑜 of a request holding [𝑡] is enabled in 𝑟 only while 𝑝 holds its approval class on
[𝑡] or its request class on the request, which by the safety case is while a request propose(𝑁 ) that
puts up a Request to 𝑜 of a request holding [𝑡] is read or held at 𝑝; a held request is put up again
after every dispatch, and its dispatch at a configuration where 𝑝 is free for its classes and ready
for a part it names takes, among the Request transactions it puts up, finitely many, the one it has
taken least recently; so if such a Request is enabled in infinitely many configurations of 𝑟 , the
agent takes one at infinitely many of these dispatches, unless a peer its condition tests is known
in 𝑟 and not yet at the agent, which happens finitely often. A Propose is one of the agent’s own
transactions, and its condition’s value at the agent changes only as the agent goal reads a trigger or
takes a transaction, after each of which it reads an own trigger; so if a Propose of 𝑝 for an attempt
of a part [𝑡] 𝑗 is enabled in infinitely many configurations of 𝑟 , its condition holds at infinitely many
own triggers, and it is taken in infinitely many, each own trigger at which it holds taking it or one
of the finitely many classes that have waited longer. So 𝑟 is a correct run of P under its fairness
condition.                                                                                               □
   Proposition 5.20 (Grassroots Compilation). For every effective local volition-guarded speci-
fication with acquaintance parts guarded by every two agents, and computable introduction rule or
none, the protocol of the compilation of its canonical platform is grassroots (Definition A.3).
   Proof. Fix the specification, the rule, its canonical platform Θ and 𝑀 = ⌈Θ⌉, and write F for
the protocol of 𝑀. It is a transactions-based protocol [53]: its transactions are those of vmaGLP
over 𝑀 (Definition 5.8), each over its participants, and its local states over 𝑃 grow with 𝑃. In a run
of F (𝑃) every variable at an agent of 𝑃 is a variable of an agent of 𝑃: those of the initial goals are,
a Reduce, an Ask and an Answer at 𝑝 take new ones from V̂𝑝 (Definitions 4.7, 5.6 and 5.7), and
Communicate and Cold-call move variables among the agents of 𝑃.
   Oblivious. Let 𝑃, 𝑃 ′ ⊂ Π be disjoint and nonempty, and 𝑒 an interleaving of a correct run of F (𝑃)
and a correct run of F (𝑃 ′ ). Reduce, Ask and Answer are unary. A Communicate between 𝑝 ∈ 𝑃
and 𝑞 ∈ 𝑃 ′ requires a variable pair shared by the two, whose variables would be of an agent of 𝑃
and of an agent of 𝑃 ′ , and these sets are disjoint; and a Cold-call from 𝑝 to 𝑞 requires a message to
𝑞 on the network output stream of 𝑝, an identifier of an agent not of 𝑃, and likewise from 𝑞 to 𝑝. So
no class whose transactions have participants in both 𝑃 and 𝑃 ′ is enabled in 𝑒. And 𝑒 is proper, the
two runs being proper and a clause renamed at 𝑝 into V̂𝑝 having no variable of an agent of 𝑃 ′ . By
the obliviousness condition of [53], 𝑒 is a correct run of F (𝑃 ∪ 𝑃 ′ ).
   Interactive. Let 𝑄 ⊂ Π, 𝑃, 𝑃 ′ ⊆ 𝑄 disjoint and nonempty, 𝑝 ∈ 𝑃 and 𝑞 ∈ 𝑃 ′ , and let 𝑟ˆ be a prefix
of a correct run of F (𝑄) with no interaction between 𝑃 and 𝑃 ′ (Definition A.2). Extend 𝑟ˆ by the
reductions and the Ask at 𝑝 by which the person of 𝑝 comes to hold an open request question, the
agent goal asking one when it starts and the next when it reads the answer to the last (Section 5.4);
by the person’s Answer to it, a request of an acquaintance part with 𝑞, one being guarded by 𝑝
and 𝑞; and by the reductions by which the agent goal reads that answer and sends 𝑞 hello, unless
it has sent one, which is then on its network output stream, undelivered, 𝑟ˆ having no interaction
between 𝑃 and 𝑃 ′ . The Cold-call of that message from 𝑝 to 𝑞 changes the states of both, and no
transaction of F (𝑃) advances the network output stream of 𝑝 past a message to an agent not of
𝑃, so the Cold-call is an interaction between 𝑃 and 𝑃 ′ . The result is a finite proper safe run, and
taking the classes enabled in it in turn, in a fair order over the countably many, each agent taking
new variables from its own set, extends it to a correct run of F (𝑄), which contains the interaction:
Volition Elicitation                                                                                 75


a Reduce class stays enabled until taken (Proposition 5.12), and so does a Communicate, an Ask
and a Cold-call class.
   F is thus oblivious and interactive, hence grassroots.                                    □
  Proposition 6.12 (Grassroots Cascade). For every effective local volition-guarded specification
with acquaintance parts guarded by every two agents, and computable introduction rule or none, the
protocol of GLP with persons over ⌈⌈Θ⌉⌉ is grassroots.
  Proof. As that of Proposition 5.20, with GLP with persons over ⌈⌈Θ⌉⌉ in place of vmaGLP over
⌈Θ⌉: a grant is unary and its new writer is a variable of its agent (Definition 6.8), and the person of
𝑝 answers the request question with a request of an acquaintance part with 𝑞 by granting it on its
construct, which the dispatcher routes and the construct process binds (Section 6.3).                □
   Theorem 6.3 (Elicitation Completeness). In every configuration of a safe run of the vmaGLP
transition system over 𝑃 and 𝑀 and every 𝑝 ∈ 𝑃, a term offered for a question 𝑋 at 𝑝 is an answer to
𝑋 that the Answer transition allows; and if the widget of the construct of 𝑋 is complete, every answer
the Answer transition allows for 𝑋 is offered.
    Proof. A construct is presented at 𝑝 only for a question 𝑋 that 𝑝’s person holds (Definition 6.1).
A submission 𝑡 it forms is a term of the type of 𝑋 : a widget forms only terms of its type, the forming
procedure of each construct process being typed (Section 6.3). The person’s input fills the positions
the person writes, but for the stream and list positions, at which the widget places new readers
or, for a list the person ends, []; and the positions the program writes hold new writers, whose
readers the construct holds. So 𝑡 is ground at the positions the person writes but for the stream and
list positions, which is an answer the Answer transition allows for 𝑋 (Definition 5.7). Conversely,
if the widget of the construct of 𝑋 is complete, every answer the Answer transition allows for 𝑋 is
formed by some input: it is offered.                                                                  □
   The mapping for Theorem 6.10 rests on what the dispatcher and the construct processes do.
Here is the dispatcher, as the repository holds it in programs/vglp/dispatcher.glp, which the
compilation emits into every compiled program under fresh names. dispatch reads the ask stream
and the person channel; fair merges the asks, the person’s grants and the constructs’ draws into
one stream, taking them in turn, and serve reads that stream and numbers the questions from 1. A
program that declares Output is dispatched by the second dispatch, with the draws of construct
0, on which the compiled program draws its output to its person; merge_draws merges them with
the constructs’ draws, and the merged stream never ends. On an ask, serve writes on its Spawn
stream the question with a fresh identifier N, the reader of a new grant stream and the writer of
a new draw stream, and the compiled program’s constructs/1 reads that stream and calls the
construct process of the question’s type; it routes each grant input(Id, P, R) to the construct
with identifier Id; it passes the constructs’ draws on; and it moves a withdrawn construct’s grant
writer to Deads, which it never reads, since the person channel never closes.
Inputs(T) ::= [Input(T) | Inputs(T)].
Route(T) ::= route(Integer, Inputs(T)?).
Routes(T) ::= [] ; [Route(T) | Routes(T)].
Deads(T) ::= [] ; [Inputs(T)? | Deads(T)].
Ask(Q) ::= ask(Constant, Q).
Spawn(Q, T) ::= spawn(Integer, Q, Inputs(T), Stream(Draw(T))?).

exported procedure(Q, T) dispatch(Stream(Ask(Q))?, PersonChannel(T)?,
    Channel(Stream(Input(T)), Stream(Draw(T))), Stream(Spawn(Q, T))).
dispatch(Asks, ch(PIn, POut?), ch(MIn?, _), Ss?) :-
    split_grants(PIn?, Inputs, MIn),
    fair(ask, Asks?, Inputs?, COut?, Es),
    serve(Es?, COut, POut, [], [], 1, Ss).
76                                                                          Idit Keidar and Ehud Shapiro



exported procedure(Q, T) dispatch(Stream(Ask(Q))?, PersonChannel(T)?,
    Stream(Input(T)), Stream(Draw(T))?, Stream(Spawn(Q, T))).
dispatch(Asks, ch(PIn, POut?), MIn?, ODs, Ss?) :-
    split_grants(PIn?, Inputs, MIn),
    merge_draws(ODs?, DOut?, POut),
    fair(ask, Asks?, Inputs?, COut?, Es),
    serve(Es?, COut, DOut, [], [], 1, Ss).

procedure(T) merge_draws(Stream(Draw(T))?, PersonOut(T)?, PersonOut(T)).
merge_draws([X | Xs], Ys, [X? | Zs?]) :- merge_draws_(Ys?, Xs?, Zs).
merge_draws(Xs, [Y | Ys], [Y? | Zs?]) :- merge_draws(Xs?, Ys?, Zs).
merge_draws([], Ys, Ys?).

procedure(T) merge_draws_(PersonOut(T)?, Stream(Draw(T))?, PersonOut(T)).
merge_draws_([X | Xs], Ys, [X? | Zs?]) :- merge_draws(Ys?, Xs?, Zs).
merge_draws_(Xs, [Y | Ys], [Y? | Zs?]) :- merge_draws_(Xs?, Ys?, Zs).
merge_draws_(Xs, [], Xs?).

procedure(T) split_grants(PersonIn(T)?, Inputs(T), Stream(Input(T))).
split_grants([input(Id, P, R) | Gs], [input(Id?, P?, R?) | Is?], Ms?) :-
    split_grants(Gs?, Is, Ms).

Event(Q, T) ::= Ask(Q) ; Input(T) ; Draw(T).
Events(Q, T) ::= [Event(Q, T) | Events(Q, T)].
Turn ::= ask ; grant ; draw.

procedure(Q, T) fair(Turn?, Stream(Ask(Q))?, Inputs(T)?, Stream(Draw(T))?,
    Events(Q, T)).
fair(ask, [A | As], Is, Ds, Es?) :- Es = [A? | Es1?], fair(grant, As?, Is?, Ds?, Es1).
fair(grant, As, [I | Is], Ds, Es?) :- Es = [I? | Es1?], fair(draw, As?, Is?, Ds?, Es1).
fair(draw, As, Is, [D | Ds], Es?) :- Es = [D? | Es1?], fair(ask, As?, Is?, Ds?, Es1).
fair(ask, As, [I | Is], Ds, Es?) :- Es = [I? | Es1?], fair(draw, As?, Is?, Ds?, Es1).
fair(grant, As, Is, [D | Ds], Es?) :- Es = [D? | Es1?], fair(ask, As?, Is?, Ds?, Es1).
fair(draw, [A | As], Is, Ds, Es?) :- Es = [A? | Es1?], fair(grant, As?, Is?, Ds?, Es1).
fair(ask, As, Is, [D | Ds], Es?) :- Es = [D? | Es1?], fair(ask, As?, Is?, Ds?, Es1).
fair(grant, [A | As], Is, Ds, Es?) :- Es = [A? | Es1?], fair(grant, As?, Is?, Ds?, Es1).
fair(draw, As, [I | Is], Ds, Es?) :- Es = [I? | Es1?], fair(draw, As?, Is?, Ds?, Es1).

procedure(Q, T) serve(Events(Q, T)?, Stream(Draw(T)), PersonOut(T),
    Routes(T)?, Deads(T)?, Integer?, Stream(Spawn(Q, T))).
serve([ask(_, Q) | Es], CInto?, DOut?, Rs, Xs, N,
      [spawn(N?, Q?, Gs?, Ds) | Ss?]) :-
    ground(N?) |
    merge(Ds?, CInto1?, CInto),
    N1 := N? + 1,
    serve(Es?, CInto1, DOut, [route(N?, Gs) | Rs?], Xs?, N1?, Ss).
serve([input(Id, P, R) | Es], CInto?, DOut?, Rs, Xs, N, Ss?) :-
    route_grant(input(Id?, P?, R?), Rs?, Rs1),
    serve(Es?, CInto, DOut, Rs1?, Xs?, N?, Ss).
serve([draw(Id, W, V) | Es], CInto?, [draw(Id?, W?, V?) | DOut?], Rs, Xs, N,
      Ss?) :-
    serve(Es?, CInto, DOut, Rs?, Xs?, N?, Ss).
serve([withdraw(Id) | Es], CInto?, [withdraw(Id?) | DOut?], Rs, Xs, N, Ss?) :-
    ground(Id?) |
    remove_route(Id?, Rs?, Rs1, Xs?, Xs1),
    serve(Es?, CInto, DOut, Rs1?, Xs1?, N?, Ss).
Volition Elicitation                                                                               77



procedure(T) route_grant(Input(T)?, Routes(T)?, Routes(T)).
route_grant(input(Id, P, R), [route(Id1, [input(Id?, P?, R?) | Gs1?]) | Rs],
            [route(Id1?, Gs1) | Rs?]) :-
    Id? =?= Id1? | true.
route_grant(G, [route(Id1, Gs?) | Rs], [route(Id1?, Gs) | Rs1?]) :-
    otherwise |
    route_grant(G?, Rs?, Rs1).
route_grant(_, [], []).

procedure(T) remove_route(Integer?, Routes(T)?, Routes(T), Deads(T)?,
    Deads(T)).
remove_route(Id, [route(Id1, Gs?) | Rs], Rs?, Xs, [Gs | Xs?]) :-
    Id? =?= Id1? | true.
remove_route(Id, [route(Id1, Gs?) | Rs], [route(Id1?, Gs) | Rs1?], Xs,
             Xs1?) :-
    otherwise |
    remove_route(Id?, Rs?, Rs1, Xs?, Xs1).
remove_route(_, [], [], Xs, Xs?).

  The dispatcher binds only variables of its own streams. The program’s end of the person channel
receives no grant, the grants going to the construct processes, and is typed Stream(Input(T)) all
the same, and a program that defines no Output has its unused output typed Stream(Draw(T)),
so the dispatcher, and every program that imports it, has no open type.

The construct process. The construct process of an ask ask(𝑇 , 𝑡(𝑌 )) with identifier Id is a goal
holding 𝑌 , the person’s end of the interactive variable — its writer where 𝑇 is in reader mode, and
its reader where 𝑇 is in writer mode — a stream of grants and a stream of draws. It holds a set of
questions, each at its path from the root of the interactive variable’s term: initially 𝑌 at the root
where 𝑇 is in reader mode, and none otherwise; as the program’s output arrives on a reader it holds,
each writer inside it at a position the person writes is added to its questions.
   On a grant input(Id, Pos, 𝑅) for a question 𝑊 it holds and has drawn, at path Pos, of type 𝑇 ′ ,
the construct process forms 𝑅 by the forming procedure of 𝑇 ′ , which yields formed(𝑢) or refused;
𝑢 is a term of type 𝑇 ′ with 𝑅 at the positions the person writes and with fresh variables where
Answer has them (Definition 5.7). On formed(𝑢) it binds 𝑊 to 𝑢, removes 𝑊 from its questions
and adds to them the writers of the new variables at the stream and list positions of 𝑢, and it holds
the readers of the new variables at the positions the program writes. A question 𝑊 it holds whose
writer is widowed, its reader dropped by the program, it removes from its questions and draws
no more, binding nothing. On refused, and on a grant for a path at which it holds no question, it
binds nothing. On refresh, which the dispatcher passes to every construct process, it draws again
what it last drew. It binds no variable but its questions, and those only on a grant.
   A construct process has the bridge draw the widget of 𝑇 once every reader in the output it holds is
assigned, with that output and its questions, and again as output arrives inside the person’s answers,
each position there not yet written whole drawn as pending; it withdraws once it holds no question
and no reader at a position whose output may hold one. The output, construct 0, is drawn element
by element as it arrives, its unwritten tail not drawn, and on refresh its last element is drawn
again. It draws draw(Id, 𝑊 , 𝑉 ), 𝑊 the widget of 𝑇 , named(𝑊 ′ , 𝐷) where a declaration gives it
𝑊 ′ , 𝐷 the default widget of 𝑇 , and 𝑉 the output it has drawn, with input(Pos) at each question
and pending(Pos) at each widget position not yet written whole, Pos the position’s path, and with
𝑉 under the functor 𝑡 of its ask; the output thread’s value is under the functor output. 𝑉 is of the
view type of 𝑇 : 𝑇 with the type 𝑆 of each position the person writes replaced by 𝑆 ; input(Path),
78                                                                                                 Idit Keidar and Ehud Shapiro


and of each position the program writes inside the person’s answers by 𝑆 ; pending(Path), so no
value drawn is typed by a wildcard.
    So the values a program draws are of one type, the union of these, and the grants’ raw inputs
are of the types of its questions, the raw input for a question whose type has positions the program
writes being a term of that type with a hole at each of them; and the term 𝑊 ′ a widget declaration
names is a name or a name applied to arguments, as the request type’s menu is applied to its
buttons and fields; the compilation forms the union of the three, the parameter T of the program’s
person channel (Definition 6.8), so the widget a construct draws is typed as its value is. The union
is formed over the alternatives of these types, an alternative common to two of them taken once,
and the compilation refuses a program two of whose alternatives have one functor and arity and
differ, since the alternatives of a union have distinct functors [59].
    The mapping for Theorem 6.10 reads the source configuration off the compiled goals and the
construct processes. An asking clause passes each argument of the goal it reduces to the goal it
calls through a variable pair of its own, 𝑆𝑙 and 𝑆𝑙 ?, and the mapping reads the pair as the argument:
at an input position the writer mgu binds 𝑆𝑙 to the goal’s term 𝑢 and the call receives 𝑆𝑙 ?, with
𝑆𝑙 ? := 𝑢 pending, and at an output position it binds the goal’s writer 𝑊 to 𝑆𝑙 ? and the call receives
𝑆𝑙 , with 𝑊 ? := 𝑆𝑙 ? pending.

   Definition B.2 (Compilation Mapping: Goals). Given a configuration of GLP with persons over 𝑃
and ⌈𝑀⌉, the mapping 𝜎 yields the vmaGLP configuration over 𝑃 and 𝑀 with, at each agent, the
goals 𝐺 of this definition and the held variables 𝐻 and the readers substitution 𝜎𝑟 of the next two:
𝐺 is the multiset of the agent’s goals, each read back to its source with its ask stream dropped:
𝑞(𝑆 1, . . . , 𝑆𝑛 , 𝐷) as the 𝑛-ary goal 𝑞(𝑆 1, . . . , 𝑆𝑛 ), 𝑞 1 (𝑆 1, . . . , 𝑆𝑛 , 𝑋 ?, 𝐷) and 𝑞 1 (𝑆 1, . . . , 𝑆𝑛 , 𝑋, 𝐷) as the
asked goal, with the identifier of the goal its asking clause reduced, and any other compiled goal as
itself; the construct processes, the dispatcher and the merge goals the compilation adds contributing
nothing; and in every goal an asking clause’s pair for an argument read as that argument: at an
input position 𝑆𝑙 ? as the term 𝑢, until the assignment is communicated, and at an output position
𝑆𝑙 as 𝑊 and 𝑆𝑙 ? as 𝑊 ?.

  Definition B.3 (Compilation Mapping: Held Variables). 𝐻 is the set of variables held for the person,
each with its type: by an ask the dispatcher has not yet taken, and by a construct process that has
not drawn its question, the person’s end of the interactive variable; by a construct process that
has drawn it, the writers of the positions the person writes, and the readers of the positions the
program writes inside the person’s answers whose output it has not drawn; the writer of each
question a construct process removed as widowed, or bound after its reader was dropped; and the
output the construct processes have drawn, shown, each reader in it replaced by its value.

   Definition B.4 (Compilation Mapping: Substitution). 𝜎𝑟 is the restriction of the readers substitution
to the readers of source variables — the variables of the initial goal, of the compiled clauses of 𝑀 as
renamed, but for their ask streams, the interactive variables, and the variables of the terms construct
processes bind questions with — less the assignment to the reader of a question bound after its
reader was dropped, and less the assignments the asking clauses’ reductions make, but for the
assignment 𝑊 ? := 𝑆𝑙 ? of an output position, which it reads through, absent while 𝑆𝑙 ? is unassigned
and 𝑊 ? := 𝑢 once 𝑆𝑙 ? := 𝑢 is; and with each assignment to a reader a construct process holds that
a Communicate has delivered to it and the construct process has not yet drawn, Communicate
removing the assignment it delivers (Definition 4.10).

  Theorem 6.10 (Compilation). The implementation of vGLP by GLP is correct, and complete for
every program whose interactive types have complete widgets.
Volition Elicitation                                                                                                                    79


   Proof. Fix a vGLP program 𝑀 and 𝑃 ⊂ Π, and let 𝜎 be the mapping of Definition B.2. 𝜎 maps
the initial configuration to the initial vmaGLP configuration: the goals read back to the initial goal
of 𝑀, and there is no construct process.
   Safety, per transition of GLP with persons over ⌈𝑀⌉. A grant, a reduction of the dispatcher or of
a merge goal the compilation adds, a reduction of a construct process that neither binds a question
nor draws output, and a Communicate on the person channel, on an ask stream, or between the
dispatcher and the construct processes change nothing 𝜎 reads: stutters. A reduction of the asking
clause of 𝑞 replaces 𝑞(𝑆 1, . . . , 𝑆𝑛 , 𝐷) by the goal 𝑞 1 (𝑆 1′ , . . . , 𝑆𝑛′ , 𝑋 ?, 𝐷 ′ ) or 𝑞 1 (𝑆 1′ , . . . , 𝑆𝑛′ , 𝑋, 𝐷 ′ ), holding
one end of a fresh interactive variable of the interactive type of 𝑞, which 𝜎 reads, through the pairs
of its arguments and with the identifier of the goal reduced, as 𝑞(𝑆 1, . . . , 𝑆𝑛 , 𝑋 ?) or 𝑞(𝑆 1, . . . , 𝑆𝑛 , 𝑋 ),
the assignments the reduction makes being outside 𝜎𝑟 , and writes on its ask stream an ask holding
the other end: the Ask of the source goal. A Communicate of an assignment an asking clause’s
reduction made replaces a pair by what 𝜎 already read it as: a stutter. A construct process binding a
question 𝑋 with the term 𝑡 its person’s input forms, a term of the type of 𝑋 ground at the positions
the person writes but for the stream and list positions, which hold readers of new writers it holds
or, for a list the person ends, [], with new writers at the positions the program writes, whose
readers it holds: the Answer of 𝑝 to 𝑋 with 𝑡, where 𝑋 is open, its reader not dropped, and a stutter
where it is not, the binding reaching no goal and 𝜎 keeping 𝑋 held. A construct process removing
a widowed question: a stutter, 𝜎 keeping its writer held, closed. A reduction of a compiled clause of
𝑀: its guard and body are those of the source clause, with calls of volitional procedures read back,
ask streams dropped and the merge goals the compilation adds contributing nothing; its added head
argument, the ask stream, matches the goal’s writer whatever it is; and the earlier compiled clauses
of the procedure, the source’s in the same order, fail or suspend on the goal: a clause that reads an
argument whose pair’s assignment, made by the asking clause, is not yet communicated suspends
on it and is followed by no clause that does not read it (Definition 5.2), so neither the reducing
clause nor an earlier one reads such an argument, and each earlier clause fails or suspends on the
source goal too; so it maps to the Reduce of the source goal with the source clause. A Communicate
to a goal of the program, within the agent or from another, maps to the source Communicate of the
same assignment, and a Cold-call is carried unchanged. A Communicate to a construct process is a
stutter, 𝜎𝑟 keeping the assignment it delivers until the construct process draws it, and a construct
process’s draw of output it holds, every reader in it assigned, is the source Communicate of that
output to the person (Definition 5.5), which removes from 𝜎𝑟 the assignments it draws; its drawing
again, on refresh, what it drew is a stutter.
   Every safe run therefore maps to a safe run. Correctness: let 𝜌 be a proper correct run of GLP
with persons over ⌈𝑀⌉ and suppose a class in the domain of the equivalence is enabled in every
configuration of a suffix of 𝜎 (𝜌) with no member taken. An Ask class of an 𝑛-ary goal corresponds
to its goal 𝑞(𝑆 1, . . . , 𝑆𝑛 , 𝐷), whose asking clause succeeds throughout, the goal holding a writer
at each output position (Definition 5.2); a Reduce class of an asked goal or of an ordinary goal
corresponds to the compiled clause, which succeeds throughout by the correspondence of the safety
case once the Communicates of the assignments its asking clause made, each enabled until taken,
are taken; a Communicate class of an assignment to a reader the person holds corresponds to the
construct process’s draw of it, enabled once the Communicates to it of that assignment and of those
read through it, each enabled until taken, are taken, and any other Communicate or Cold-call class
to itself. Each is enabled in the corresponding suffix of 𝜌, whose liveness takes it, and its image is a
member. Hence 𝜎 (𝜌) is correct.
   Completeness, for a program whose interactive types have complete widgets. Let 𝑟 be a correct
run of the vmaGLP transition system over 𝑃 and 𝑀. We build a proper correct run 𝑟 ′ of GLP with
persons over ⌈𝑀⌉ with 𝜎 (𝑟 ′ ) = 𝑟 , taking the transitions of 𝑟 in turn. For an Ask, reduce the goal’s
80                                                                             Idit Keidar and Ehud Shapiro


asking clause, let the merge goals carry its ask to the dispatcher, and let the dispatcher spawn its
construct process, stutters but the first. For an Answer to 𝑋 with 𝑡, the widget being complete,
grant the input that forms 𝑡, let the dispatcher route it, and let the construct process bind 𝑋 with 𝑡,
stutters but the last. For a Reduce, take the compiled clause, and, where it drops the reader of a
question, the construct process’s removal of it, a stutter. For a Communicate to the person, take the
Communicates to the construct process of its assignment and of those read through it, stutters,
and the construct process’s draw of the output. Any other Communicate, and a Cold-call, of 𝑟 is
taken as it is, followed by the Communicates on the channels of the dispatcher that it enables. The
run 𝑟 ′ is proper, its variables fresh, and live: a construct process awaiting input is suspended, the
dispatcher and the merge goals are reduced in the stage that enables them, and every other class
enabled from some configuration on is of a source class that 𝑟 takes, 𝑟 being correct. Hence 𝜎 is
complete.                                                                                            □
   Theorem 6.11 (Cascade). For every effective local volition-guarded specification, computable
introduction rule or none, and 𝑃 ⊂ Π, 𝜎1 ◦ 𝜎2 ◦ 𝜎3 is a correct implementation of F (𝑃) by GLP with
persons over 𝑃 and ⌈⌈Θ⌉⌉, under the fairness condition that a run 𝜌 is proper, that no approval question
stays open in 𝜎3 (𝜌), and that 𝜎2 (𝜎3 (𝜌)) is connected, unobstructed, steady and resolute.
   Proof. Fix an effective local volition-guarded specification and 𝑃 ⊂ Π, and let 𝜌 be a correct run
of GLP with persons over 𝑃 and ⌈⌈Θ⌉⌉ under the fairness condition. By Theorem 6.10, 𝜎3 (𝜌) is a
correct, proper run of the vmaGLP transition system over 𝑃 and ⌈Θ⌉, in which no approval question
stays open; by Theorem 5.19, 𝜎2 (𝜎3 (𝜌)), being connected, unobstructed, steady and resolute, is a
correct run of P under its fairness condition; and by Theorem 3.24, 𝜎1 (𝜎2 (𝜎3 (𝜌))) is a correct run
of F (𝑃). 𝜎1 ◦ 𝜎2 ◦ 𝜎3 takes the initial configuration to the initial configuration, as each of its parts
does, so it is a correct implementation.                                                               □
